.

Proof of Proposition
6.1
.

In this proof we let
c,C>0
be small and large constants which depend on
N
, and the values can change from line to line.

We will show that
\hat{\eta}_{t}^{-}
equals
\hat{\eta}_{t+s}^{+}
in
\llbracket-N,N+1\rrbracket
with probability
>1-C(s/t)^{1/30}
, assuming that
t,s
are large enough depending on
N
. We could also assume that
t/s
is large enough depending on
N
, since otherwise we would have
1-C(s/t)^{1/30}<0
.
For the parameters in the definition of the events
\mathcal{A}
,
\mathcal{B}_{-}
,
\mathcal{B}_{+}
, and
\mathcal{F}_{t}
, we take
m=\lfloor t/10\rfloor
and
r=\lfloor s^{1/3}t^{1/3}\rfloor
.
Denote
v_{1}=\left(-\lfloor r\rho^{-2}(1-\rho)^{-2}\rfloor,0\right)
and
v_{2}=\left(0,-\lfloor r\rho^{-2}(1-\rho)^{-2}\rfloor\right)
.

We first lower bound
\mathbb{P}[\mathcal{B}_{-}]
.
We take
u_{-,1}
to be the last vertex in
\Gamma_{v_{1}}^{-}\cap I_{0}^{-}
, and
u_{-,2}
to be the last vertex in
\Gamma_{v_{2}}^{-}\cap I_{0}^{-}
.
Then
u_{-,1}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\leq 0}\times\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
, and
u_{-,2}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}\times\mathbb{Z}_{\leq 0}
.
By Corollary
5.8
, we have

\mathbb{P}[ad(u_{-,1})<-r],\;\mathbb{P}[ad(u_{-,2})>r]>1-Ce^{-cr},

and

\mathbb{P}[u_{-,1}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\leq 0}\times\llbracket 0,2r(1-\rho)^{-4}\rrbracket],\;\mathbb{P}[u_{-,2}\in\llbracket 0,2r\rho^{-4}\rrbracket\times\mathbb{Z}_{\leq 0}]>1-Ce^{-cr}.

See Figure
10(a)
for an illustration.
Since
2r(1-\rho)^{-4},2r\rho^{-4}<2m
(as
t,s,t/s
are large enough), we have
\mathbb{P}[d(u_{-,1}),d(u_{-,2})<2m]>1-Ce^{-cr}
.
By Proposition
5.5
we have
\mathbb{P}[\Gamma_{v_{1}}^{-}\cap\mathbb{L}_{m}=\Gamma_{v_{2}}^{-}\cap\mathbb{L}_{m}]>1-Crm^{-2/3}
.
Thus we conclude that
\mathbb{P}[\mathcal{B}_{-}]>1-Crm^{-2/3}-Ce^{-cr}
.

We next bound
\mathbb{P}[\mathcal{A}\setminus\mathcal{B}_{+}]
(see Figure
10(b)
for several sets in
\mathbb{Z}^{2}
to be defined). We take
u_{+,1}
to be the last vertex in
\Gamma_{v_{1}}^{+}\cap I_{s}^{+}
, and
u_{+,2}
to be the last vertex in
\Gamma_{v_{2}}^{+}\cap I_{s}^{+}
.
Then by ordering of geodesics (Lemma
5.1
), and that all of
p_{s}^{+}
,
u_{+,1}
, and
u_{+,2}
are in
\partial I_{s}^{+}
, we must have that
u_{+,1}\in p_{s}^{+}+\mathbb{Z}_{\leq 0}\times\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
and
u_{+,2}\in p_{s}^{+}+\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}\times\mathbb{Z}_{\leq 0}
.

Let
A=\mathbb{V}_{(-r,r)}\cap\mathbb{H}_{(s-(r^{3}s^{-2})s^{1/3},s+(r^{3}s^{-2})s^{1/3})}
.
Note that
p_{s}^{+}
is the last vertex in
\{u\in\Gamma_{\mathbf{0}}^{+}:\mathbf{G}^{+}(u)\leq s\}
, so by Lemma
3.1
we have that
p_{s}^{+}-(1,0)
or
p_{s}^{+}-(0,1)
is the last vertex in
\{u\in\Gamma_{\mathbf{0}}^{+}:T_{\mathbf{0},u}^{+}\leq s\}
.
Then by Corollary
5.11
we have
\mathbb{P}[p_{s}^{+}\in A]>1-Ce^{-cr^{3}s^{-2}}
.

•

When
p_{s}^{+}\in A
, we must have
ad(u_{+,1})<ad(p_{s}^{+})-r
and
ad(u_{+,2})>ad(p_{s}^{+})+r
, unless
u_{+,1}\in A^{\prime}
or
u_{+,2}\in A^{\prime}
, where

A^{\prime}=A+\{u\in\mathbb{R}^{2}:|d(u)|,|ad(u)|\leq r\}\subset\mathbb{V}_{(-2r,2r)}\cap\mathbb{H}_{(s-Cr,s+Cr)}.

By Corollary
5.8
, we have

\mathbb{P}[u_{+,1}\in A^{\prime}],\mathbb{P}[u_{+,2}\in A^{\prime}]<Ce^{-cr^{3}s^{-2}}
.
Recall that under
\mathcal{A}
we have
ad(p_{s}^{+})=ad(p^{*})
, we now conclude that

\mathbb{P}[\{ad(u_{+,1})>ad(p^{*})-r\}\cap\mathcal{A}],\;\mathbb{P}[\{ad(u_{+,2})<ad(p^{*})+r\}\cap\mathcal{A}]<Ce^{-cr^{3}s^{-2}}.

•

When
p_{s}^{+}\in A
, we must also have
d(u_{+,1})<d(p_{s}^{+})+2r(1-\rho)^{-4}
, unless
u_{+,1}\in A+\mathbb{Z}_{\leq 0}\times\llbracket 2r(1-\rho)^{-4},\infty\rrbracket
.
By Corollary
5.8
this happens with probability
<Ce^{-cr^{3}s^{-2}}
, so we have

\mathbb{P}[d(u_{+,1}-p_{s}^{+})\geq 2r(1-\rho)^{-4}]<Ce^{-cr^{3}s^{-2}}.

When
\mathcal{A}
holds, we can find some
w_{1},w_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}
,
|ad(w_{1})|,|ad(w_{2})|\leq r+1
, such that
w_{1}\in(\mathbb{Z}_{-}\times\mathbb{Z}_{+})\cap(p_{s}^{+}-p^{*}+\mathbb{Z}_{-}\times\mathbb{Z}_{+})
, and
w_{2}\in(\mathbb{Z}_{+}\times\mathbb{Z}_{-})\cap(p_{s}^{+}-p^{*}+\mathbb{Z}_{+}\times\mathbb{Z}_{-})
. This implies that
|d(p_{s}^{+}-p^{*})|\leq 2r
.
Thus we conclude that

\mathbb{P}[\{d(u_{+,1}-p^{*})\geq 2m\}\cap\mathcal{A}]\leq\mathbb{P}[d(u_{+,1}-p_{s}^{+})\geq 2r(1-\rho)^{-4}]<Ce^{-cr^{3}s^{-2}}.

Here the first inequality is by
2m-2r\geq 2r(1-\rho)^{-4}
(due to taking
s,t,t/s
large).
Similarly we have
\mathbb{P}[\{d(u_{+,2}-p^{*})\geq 2m\}\cap\mathcal{A}]<Ce^{-cr^{3}s^{-2}}
.

•

We have shown that
\mathcal{A}
implies
|d(p_{s}^{+}-p^{*})|\leq 2r
. If in addition
p_{s}^{+}\in A
, we must have that
d(p^{*})\geq d(p_{s}^{+})-2r>0
(since
s,t,t/s
are taken to be large). So using Proposition
5.5
we have

\mathbb{P}[\{(\Gamma_{v_{1}}^{+}-p^{*})\cap\mathbb{L}_{m}\neq(\Gamma_{v_{2}}^{+}-p^{*})\cap\mathbb{L}_{m}\}\cap\mathcal{A}]\leq\mathbb{P}[\Gamma_{v_{1}}^{+}\cap\mathbb{L}_{m}\neq\Gamma_{v_{2}}^{+}\cap\mathbb{L}_{m}]+\mathbb{P}[p_{s}^{+}\not\in A]\\
<Crm^{-2/3}+Ce^{-cr^{3}s^{-2}}.

Thus we conclude that
\mathbb{P}[\mathcal{A}\setminus\mathcal{B}_{+}]<Crm^{-2/3}+Ce^{-cr^{3}s^{-2}}
.

Finally we consider
\mathbb{P}[\mathcal{F}_{t}]
.
Since
p_{t}^{-}
is the last vertex in
\{u\in\Gamma_{\mathbf{0}}^{-}:\mathbf{G}^{-}(u)\leq t\}
, by Lemma
3.1
we have that
p_{t}^{-}-(1,0)
or
p_{t}^{-}-(0,1)
is the last vertex in
\{u\in\Gamma_{\mathbf{0}}^{-}:T_{\mathbf{0},u}^{-}\leq t\}
.
Then by Corollary
5.11
, with probability
>1-Ce^{-ct^{2/3}}
we have that
p_{t}^{-}\in\mathbb{H}_{(t/2,2t)}\cap\mathbb{V}_{(-t^{8/9},t^{8/9})}
, thus
d(p_{t}^{-})\geq((1-\rho)^{2}+\rho^{2})t/2-t^{8/9}\geq t/4-t^{8/9}
.
Note that since
m=\lfloor t/10\rfloor
and
t
is taken large enough depending on
N
, we have
t/4-t^{8/9}>2m+2N+2
. So we have
\mathbb{P}[\mathcal{F}_{t}]>1-Ce^{-ct^{2/3}}
.

By Lemma
6.3
,
\hat{\eta}_{t}^{-}
equals
\hat{\eta}_{t+s}^{+}
in
\llbracket-N,N+1\rrbracket
with probability at least

\mathbb{P}[\mathcal{B}_{-}]+\mathbb{P}[\mathcal{A}]-\mathbb{P}[\mathcal{A}\setminus\mathcal{B}_{+}]+\mathbb{P}[\mathcal{F}_{t}]-2\\
>1-(Crm^{-2/3}+Ce^{-cr})-C(rs^{-2/3})^{-1/10}-(Crm^{-2/3}+Ce^{-cr^{3}s^{-2}})-Ce^{-ct^{2/3}}>1-C(s/t)^{1/30},

where the first inequality uses the estimates of
\mathbb{P}[\mathcal{B}_{-}]
,
\mathbb{P}[\mathcal{A}\setminus\mathcal{B}_{+}]
, and
\mathbb{P}[\mathcal{A}]
above, and the estimates on
\mathbb{P}[\mathcal{A}]
from Lemma
6.2
.
Thus the conclusion follows.
∎

6.1
The initial step coupling

This subsection is devoted to proving Lemma
6.2
.

We define
(\tau_{t})_{t\in\mathbb{R}}
as the process of stationary TASEP with density
\rho
, i.e. for any
t\in\mathbb{R}
, we have
\tau_{t}(x)
being Bernoulli
(\rho)
for each
x\in\mathbb{Z}
independently.
Our strategy is to construct a coupling between the processes
({\eta_{t}^{+}})_{t\geq 0}
and
(\tau_{t})_{t\geq 0}
, where (with high probability)
\hat{\eta}_{s}^{+}
and
\tau_{s}
are identical outside
\llbracket-r,r\rrbracket
, and have the same number of particles in
\llbracket-r,r\rrbracket
.
It would be straightforward to couple
\hat{\eta}_{0}^{-}
and
\tau_{s}
since both are Bernoulli
(\rho)
on
\mathbb{Z}\setminus\{0,1\}
.

We denote
\alpha=(rs^{-2/3})^{1/5}
, and
r_{i}=\alpha^{i}s^{2/3}
, for
i=1,2,3,4
.
Below we assume that
\alpha
and
s
are large enough, and also
\alpha<r^{0.01}
.
Recall the notations
L^{-}
,
\mathbf{G}^{-}
,
I_{t}^{-}
,
\partial I_{t}^{-}
,
\xi^{-,\vee}
,
\xi^{-,\vee,t}
,
T_{u,v}^{-}
,
\Gamma_{u,v}^{-}
,
\Gamma_{u}^{-}
,
\Gamma_{u}^{-,\vee}
,
p_{t}^{-}
(resp.
L^{+}
,
\mathbf{G}^{+}
,
I_{t}^{+}
,
\partial I_{t}^{+}
,
\xi^{+,\vee}
,
\xi^{+,\vee,t}
,
T_{u,v}^{+}
,
\Gamma_{u,v}^{+}
,
\Gamma_{u}^{+}
,
\Gamma_{u}^{+,\vee}
,
p_{t}^{+}
) for LPP with weights
\xi^{-}
(resp.
\xi^{+}
).
Also recall the notations
\mathbb{V}_{x}
,
\mathbb{H}_{x}
and
\mathbb{V}_{A}
,
\mathbb{H}_{A}
, for
x\in\mathbb{R}
and
A\subset\mathbb{R}
.

We now explain the coupling between the processes
({\eta_{t}^{+}})_{t\geq 0}
and
(\tau_{t})_{t\geq 0}
.
One straightforward way is to couple
\eta_{0}^{+}
and
\tau_{0}
so that they are the same outside a compact interval, and let them evolve using the same exponential waiting times (just like how
({\eta_{t}^{-}})_{t\geq 0}
and
({\eta_{s+t}^{+}})_{t\geq 0}
are coupled).
One can show that under this coupling, with high probability
\eta_{s}^{+}
and
\tau_{s}
are the same, like how Proposition
6.1
is proved assuming Lemma
6.2
.
However, we need to compare
\hat{\eta}_{s}^{+}
and
\tau_{s}
instead.
For this, we first shift
{\eta_{0}^{+}}
by
ad(p_{s}^{+})
, and then couple it with
\tau_{0}
, so that they are the same outside a compact interval, and then let them evolve using the same exponential waiting times.
One problem is that the number
ad(p_{s}^{+})
depends on the evolution of
(\eta_{t}^{+})_{t\geq 0}
.
To solve this, we exploit the fact that
ad(p_{s}^{+})
mostly depends on the evolution around the hole-particle pair.
Specifically, we take the following approach: we first sample the evolution of
({\eta_{t}^{+}})_{t\geq 0}
around the pair (which corresponds to sampling the waiting times
\xi^{+,\vee,0}
in a tube
\mathbb{V}_{(-r_{1},r_{1})}
) to get a proxy of
p_{s}^{+}
, which equals
p_{s}^{+}
with high probability.
Using that we could shift
\tau_{0}
and couple the rest of the waiting times
\xi^{+,\vee,0}
with the waiting times of
(\tau_{t})_{t\geq 0}
.

We start by defining the proxy of
p_{s}^{+}
.
Denote
P=\mathbb{V}_{(-r_{1},r_{1})}\cap\mathbb{Z}^{2}
.
First we define
L^{P}
, by letting
L^{P}(u)=0
for
u\in I_{0}^{+}\cup(\mathbb{Z}^{2}\setminus P)
, and setting
L^{P}(u)=L^{P}(u-(1,0))\vee L^{P}(u-(0,1))+\xi^{+,\vee,0}(u)
recursively for each
u\in P\setminus I_{0}^{+}
.
Like Lemma
3.5
,
L^{P}(u)
can also be defined as the maximum passage time in
\mathbb{Z}^{2}\setminus I_{0}^{+}
to
u
, under the weights
\{\xi^{+,\vee,0}(v)\mathds{1}[v\in P]\}_{v\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}\setminus I_{0}^{+}}
.
Then with probability
1
we have that
L^{P}(u)
for
u\in P\setminus I_{0}^{+}
are mutually different, and below we work under this event.
Analogue to the inductive construction of
\Gamma^{+}_{\mathbf{0}}
(see Lemma
3.2
), we define
\Gamma^{P}_{\mathbf{0}}
by letting
\Gamma^{P}_{\mathbf{0}}[1]=\mathbf{0}
and

\Gamma^{P}_{\mathbf{0}}[i+1]=\argmin_{v\in\{\Gamma^{P}_{\mathbf{0}}[i]+(1,0),\Gamma^{P}_{\mathbf{0}}[i]+(0,1)\}\cap P}L^{P}(v)

(6.8)

for each
i\in\mathbb{N}
.
Recall that
p_{s}^{+}
is the last vertex in
\Gamma^{+}_{\mathbf{0}}\cap I_{s}^{+}
.
We let
p^{P}
be the last vertex in
\{u\in\Gamma^{P}_{\mathbf{0}}:L^{P}(u)\leq s\}
.
Denote
M=ad(p^{P})
. Then
M
is determined by
I_{0}^{+}\cap P
and
\{\xi^{+,\vee,0}(u)\}_{u\in P\setminus I_{0}^{+}}
.

We next show that this proxy
p^{P}
equals
p_{s}^{+}
with high probability.

Lemma 6.5
.

\mathbb{P}[p^{P}=p_{s}^{+}]>1-Ce^{-c\alpha^{3}}
for some constants
c,C>0
.

Proof.

According to Lemma
3.5
and as above stated,
L^{+}(u)
and
L^{P}(u)
are the maximum passage times from a vertex in
\mathbb{Z}^{2}\setminus I_{0}^{+}
to
u
, under the weights
\{\xi^{+,\vee,0}(v)\}_{v\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}\setminus I_{0}^{+}}
and
\{\xi^{+,\vee,0}(v)\mathds{1}[v\in P]\}_{v\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}\setminus I_{0}^{+}}
respectively. Also Lemma
3.5
states that the path with the maximum passage time to
u
under the weights
\{\xi^{+,\vee,0}(v)\}_{v\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}\setminus I_{0}^{+}}
is precisely
\Gamma_{u}^{+,\vee}\setminus I_{0}^{+}
.
Then we have that
L^{+}(u)=L^{P}(u)
for any
u
with
\Gamma_{u}^{+,\vee}\setminus I_{0}^{+}\subset P
.

We take

u_{1}=\left(\left\lfloor-\frac{3((1-\rho)^{2}+\rho^{2})r_{1}}{4\rho^{2}}\right\rfloor,0\right),\quad u_{2}=\left(0,\left\lfloor-\frac{3((1-\rho)^{2}+\rho^{2})r_{1}}{4(1-\rho)^{2}}\right\rfloor\right).

We let
\mathcal{D}_{1}
be the event where

\Gamma_{u_{1}}^{+}\cap\mathbb{H}_{(-\infty,2s)}\subset\mathbb{V}_{(-r_{1},-r_{1}/2)},\;\;\Gamma_{u_{2}}^{+}\cap\mathbb{H}_{(-\infty,2s)}\subset\mathbb{V}_{(r_{1}/2,r_{1})}.

Assuming that
\mathcal{D}_{1}
holds, we denote

S=\big(\cup_{v\in\Gamma_{u_{1}}^{+}}(v+\mathbb{Z}_{+}\times\mathbb{Z}_{\leq 0})\big)\cap\big(\cup_{v\in\Gamma_{u_{2}}^{+}}(v+\mathbb{Z}_{\leq 0}\times\mathbb{Z}_{+})\big)\cap(\mathbb{H}_{(-\infty,2s)}\setminus I_{0}^{+}).

In other words,
S
is the set consisting of vertices in
\mathbb{H}_{(-\infty,2s)}\setminus I_{0}^{+}
between
\Gamma_{u_{1}}^{+}
and
\Gamma_{u_{2}}^{+}
.
Take any
u\in S
, by the non-crossing property (Lemma
3.3
), we must have that
\Gamma_{u}^{+,\vee}
is disjoint from
\cup_{v\in\Gamma_{u_{1}}^{+}}(v+\mathbb{Z}_{\leq 0}\times\mathbb{Z}_{+})
.
As
u_{1}+\mathbb{Z}_{\leq 0}^{2}
,
\cup_{v\in\Gamma_{u_{1}}^{+}}(v+\mathbb{Z}_{\leq 0}\times\mathbb{Z}_{+})
,
\cup_{v\in\Gamma_{u_{1}}^{+}}(v+\mathbb{Z}_{+}\times\mathbb{Z}_{\leq 0})
is a disjoint partition of
\mathbb{Z}^{2}
, and
u_{1}+\mathbb{Z}_{\leq 0}^{2}\subset I_{0}^{+}
, we must have that
\Gamma_{u}^{+,\vee}\setminus I_{0}^{+}\subset\cup_{v\in\Gamma_{u_{1}}^{+}}(v+\mathbb{Z}_{+}\times\mathbb{Z}_{\leq 0})
.
Since
u\in\mathbb{H}_{(-\infty,2s)}
, we further have that
\Gamma_{u}^{+,\vee}\setminus I_{0}^{+}\subset\cup_{v\in\Gamma_{u_{1}}^{+}\cap\mathbb{H}_{(-\infty,2s)}}(v+\mathbb{Z}_{+}\times\mathbb{Z}_{\leq 0})
.
Similarly we have
\Gamma_{u}^{+,\vee}\setminus I_{0}^{+}\subset\cup_{v\in\Gamma_{u_{2}}^{+}\cap\mathbb{H}_{(-\infty,2s)}}(v+\mathbb{Z}_{\leq 0}\times\mathbb{Z}_{+})
.
Then by
\mathcal{D}_{1}
we must have
\Gamma_{u}^{+,\vee}\setminus I_{0}^{+}\subset P
, so
L^{+}(u)=L^{P}(u)
.

Let
\mathcal{D}_{2}
be the event where
\Gamma_{\mathbf{0}}^{+}\cap I_{s}^{+}\subset\mathbb{V}_{(-r_{1}/3,r_{1}/3)}\cap\mathbb{H}_{(-\infty,3s/2)}
.
Under
\mathcal{D}_{1}
we have
\mathbb{V}_{(-r_{1}/3-1,r_{1}/3+1)}\cap\mathbb{H}_{(-\infty,2s)}\setminus I_{0}^{+}\subset S
, so under
\mathcal{D}_{1}\cap\mathcal{D}_{2}
we have
(\Gamma_{\mathbf{0}}^{+}\cap I_{s}^{+})+\{(1,0),(0,1)\}\subset S
.
Then by the inductive constructions of
\Gamma^{+}_{\mathbf{0}}
and
\Gamma_{\mathbf{0}}^{P}
(Lemma
3.2
and (
6.8
)), one can inductively show that
\Gamma_{\mathbf{0}}^{+}[i]=\Gamma_{\mathbf{0}}^{P}[i]
and
L^{+}(\Gamma_{\mathbf{0}}^{+}[i]+(1,0))=L^{P}(\Gamma_{\mathbf{0}}^{P}[i]+(1,0))
,
L^{+}(\Gamma_{\mathbf{0}}^{+}[i]+(0,1))=L^{P}(\Gamma_{\mathbf{0}}^{P}[i]+(0,1))
, for all
i\in\mathbb{N}
such that
L^{+}(\Gamma_{\mathbf{0}}^{+}[i])\leq s
.
By considering the largest such
i
we conclude that
p^{P}=p_{s}^{+}
.

Now we have
\mathbb{P}[p^{P}=p_{s}^{+}]\geq\mathbb{P}[\mathcal{D}_{1}\cap\mathcal{D}_{2}]
, and it remains to lower bound
\mathbb{P}[\mathcal{D}_{1}]
and
\mathbb{P}[\mathcal{D}_{2}]
.
By Corollary
5.8
we have
\mathbb{P}[\mathcal{D}_{1}]>1-Ce^{-cr_{1}^{3}s^{-2}}
.
For
\mathcal{D}_{2}
, as
p_{s}^{+}
is the last vertex in
\{u\in\Gamma_{\mathbf{0}}^{+}:\mathbf{G}^{+}(u)\leq s\}
, by Lemma
3.1
we have that
p_{s}^{+}-(1,0)
or
p_{s}^{+}-(0,1)
is the last vertex in
\{u\in\Gamma_{\mathbf{0}}^{+}:T_{\mathbf{0},u}^{+}\leq s\}
.
Then Corollary
5.11
implies that
\mathbb{P}[\mathcal{D}_{2}]>1-Ce^{-cr_{1}^{3}s^{-2}}
(noting that
3s/2>s+C(r_{1}^{3}s^{-2})s^{1/3}
by our choice of the parameters). Thus the conclusion follows.
∎

We next couple
\tau_{0}
and
({\eta_{t}^{+}})_{t\geq 0}
.
We state the coupling by constructing
\tau_{0}
conditioned on
({\eta_{t}^{+}})_{t\geq 0}
, using the following steps.

1.

Let
\tau_{0}(x-M)
be i.i.d. Bernoulli
(\rho)
for each
x\in\llbracket-r_{2},r_{2}\rrbracket
.

2.

For each
x=\lfloor-r_{2}\rfloor,\lfloor-r_{2}\rfloor-1,\ldots
, we take
\tau_{0}(x-M)
to be i.i.d. Bernoulli
(\rho)
, until the first
x_{*}\in\mathbb{Z}
with
\sum_{x=x_{*}}^{0}{\eta_{0}^{+}}(x)-\tau_{0}(x-M)=0
.
Then for each
x<x_{*}
we take
\tau_{0}(x-M)={\eta_{0}^{+}}(x)
.

3.

For each
x=\lceil r_{2}\rceil,\lceil r_{2}\rceil+1,\ldots
, we take
\tau_{0}(x-M)
to be i.i.d. Bernoulli
(\rho)
, until the first
x^{*}\in\mathbb{Z}
with
\sum_{x=1}^{x^{*}}{\eta_{0}^{+}}(x)-\tau_{0}(x-M)=0
.
Then for each
x>x^{*}
we take
\tau_{0}(x-M)={\eta_{0}^{+}}(x)
.

As
\alpha=r_{2}/r_{1}
is large enough, the set
P\cap(\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}\times\mathbb{Z}_{\leq 0}\cup\mathbb{Z}_{\leq 0}\times\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0})
is contained in
\{u\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}:|ad(u)|<r_{2}\}
.
Also note that
I_{0}^{+}\cap P
is determined by
I_{0}^{+}\cap P\cap(\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}\times\mathbb{Z}_{\leq 0}\cup\mathbb{Z}_{\leq 0}\times\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0})
, as
P\cap\mathbb{Z}_{-}^{2}\subset I_{0}^{+}
and
P\cap\mathbb{Z}_{+}^{2}
is disjoint from
I_{0}^{+}
.
Then
I_{0}^{+}\cap P
is determined by
I_{0}^{+}\cap\{u\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}:|ad(u)|<r_{2}\}
, which (by Lemma
3.6
) is determined by
\{{\eta_{0}^{+}}(x)\}_{|x|<r_{2}}
, thus is independent of
\{{\eta_{0}^{+}}(x)\}_{|x|\geq r_{2}}
.
Since
M
is determined by
I_{0}^{+}\cap P
and
\{\xi^{+,\vee,0}(u)\}_{u\in P\setminus I_{0}^{+}}
, we have that
M
is also independent of
\{{\eta_{0}^{+}}(x)\}_{|x|\geq r_{2}}
. Then from the construction of
\tau_{0}
, and that
\{{\eta_{0}^{+}}(x)\}_{|x|\geq r_{2}}
are i.i.d. Bernoulli
(\rho)
, we have that
\{\tau_{0}(x-M)\}_{x\in\mathbb{Z}}
are i.i.d. Bernoulli
(\rho)
conditioned on
M
.
Thus
\{\tau_{0}(x)\}_{x\in\mathbb{Z}}
are i.i.d. Bernoulli
(\rho)
and independent of
M
.
Conditioned on
I_{0}^{+}
, both
\tau_{0}
and
M
are independent of
\{\xi^{+,\vee,0}(u)\}_{u\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}\setminus(P\cup I_{0}^{+})}
.

We would let
(\tau_{t})_{t\geq 0}
evolve using the waiting times
\{\xi^{+,\vee,0}(u)\}_{u\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}\setminus(P\cup I_{0}^{+})}
.
For this, in
\tau_{0}
we label the holes by
\mathbb{Z}
from left to right, and the particles by
\mathbb{Z}
from right to left, such that for any
x\in\mathbb{Z}\setminus\llbracket x_{*},x^{*}\rrbracket
, the particle (or hole) at site
x-M
has the same label as the particle (or hole) at site
x
in
{\eta_{0}^{+}}
.
This can be achieved since that
\tau_{0}
and
\eta_{0}^{+}
are the same outside
\llbracket x_{*},x^{*}\rrbracket
, and they have the same number of particles in
\llbracket x_{*},0\rrbracket
and
\llbracket 1,x^{*}\rrbracket
, respectively.

Let
L^{\tau}(a,b)
be the time when particle labeled
b
switches with the hole labeled
a
if in
\tau_{0}
this particle is to the left of this hole, and let
L^{\tau}(a,b)=0
otherwise. Note that unlike
L^{+}
, this function
L^{\tau}
does not have the same distribution as the Busemann function in LPP. However, we can still define a growth process from it. For each
t\geq 0
denote
I_{t}^{\tau}:=\{u\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}:L^{\tau}(u)\leq t\}
, and
\partial I_{t}^{\tau}:=\{u\in I_{t}^{\tau}:L^{\tau}(u+(1,0))\vee L^{\tau}(u+(0,1))>t\}
.
Then
I_{0}^{\tau}
is the same as
I_{0}^{+}
outside a compact set.

Lemma 6.6
.

For any
u\in(I_{0}^{\tau}\setminus I_{0}^{+})\cup(I_{0}^{+}\setminus I_{0}^{\tau})
we have
ad(u)\in\llbracket x_{*},x^{*}\rrbracket

Proof.

Write
u=(a,b)
.
If
u\not\in I_{0}^{+}
, there is some
x\leq 0
such that
(a-x,b-x)\not\in I_{0}^{+}
,
(a-x-1,b-x)\in I_{0}^{+}
, or
(a-x-1,b-x)\not\in I_{0}^{+}
,
(a-x-1,b-x-1)\in I_{0}^{+}
.
Then by Lemma
3.6
, either
\eta_{0}^{+}(a-b)=1
and the particle at site
a-b
(in
\eta_{0}^{+}
) has label
b-x
, or
\eta_{0}^{+}(a-b)=0
and the hole at site
a-b
(in
\eta_{0}^{+}
) has label
a-x-1
.
If
u\in I_{0}^{\tau}
, we can similar deduce that there is some
y\geq 0
such that
(a+y+1,b+y+1)\not\in I_{0}^{\tau}
,
(a+y,b+y+1)\in I_{0}^{\tau}
, or
(a+y,b+y+1)\not\in I_{0}^{\tau}
,
(a+y,b+y)\in I_{0}^{\tau}
.
Then by (an analogue of) Lemma
3.6
, either
\tau_{0}(a-b-M)=1
and the particle at site
a-b-M
(in
\tau_{0}
) has label
b+y+1
, or
\tau_{0}(a-b-M)=0
and the hole at site
a-b-M
(in
\tau_{0}
) has label
a+y
.

Thus if
u\in I_{0}^{\tau}\setminus I_{0}^{+}
, we must have
\eta_{0}^{+}(a-b)\neq\tau_{0}(a-b-M)
or the particles/holes do not have the same label.
So from the coupling between
\eta_{0}^{+}
and
\tau_{0}
we have that
a-b\in\llbracket x_{*},x^{*}\rrbracket
, and the conclusion follows. The case where
u\in I_{0}^{+}\setminus I_{0}^{\tau}
follows from similar arguments.
∎

We can also define the waiting times, by letting

\xi^{\tau,\vee}(u)=L^{\tau}(u)-L^{\tau}(u-(1,0))\vee L^{\tau}(u-(0,1))

for any
u\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}\setminus I_{0}^{\tau}
.
Given
I_{0}^{\tau}
(equivalently,
\tau_{0}
and the labels), we have that
\{\xi^{\tau,\vee}(u)\}_{u\not\in I_{0}^{\tau}}
are i.i.d.
\Exp(1)
, since they are precisely the waiting times for certain particles and holes to switch.
Then almost surely
L^{\tau}(u)
are mutually different for all
u\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}\setminus I_{0}^{\tau}
, and below we assume this event.

We now couple
\{\xi^{\tau,\vee}(u)\}_{u\not\in I_{0}^{\tau}}
with
(\eta_{t}^{+})_{\geq 0}
, such that conditioned on
\tau_{0}
and
(\eta_{t}^{+})_{t\geq 0}
, we have
\xi^{\tau,\vee}(u)=\xi^{+,\vee,0}(u)
for any
u\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}\setminus(I^{+}_{0}\cup I_{0}^{\tau}\cup P)
and
\xi^{\tau,\vee}(u)
for
u\in(P\cup I^{+}_{0})\setminus I_{0}^{\tau}
are i.i.d.
\Exp(1)
.
Under this coupling, we denote
\mathcal{E}_{1}
as the event where for any
x<-r
,
\tau_{s}(x)=\hat{\eta}_{s}^{+}(x)=\eta_{s}^{+}(x+ad(p_{s}^{+}))
,
and the particle or hole at site
x
has the same label for
\tau_{s}
and
\hat{\eta}_{s}^{+}
; denote
\mathcal{E}_{2}
as the event where the same is true for any
x>r
.

Lemma 6.7
.

We have
\mathbb{P}[\mathcal{E}_{1}],\mathbb{P}[\mathcal{E}_{2}]>1-C\alpha^{-1/2}
when
Cs^{2/3}<r<s^{2/3+0.01}
and
s>C
, where
C>0
is a constant.

We can now prove Lemma
6.2
assuming Lemma
6.7
.

Proof of Lemma
6.2
.

Under
\mathcal{E}_{1}\cap\mathcal{E}_{2}
, we have
\tau_{s}(x)=\hat{\eta}_{s}^{+}(x)
for any
x\in\mathbb{Z},|x|>r
.
Also, note
1+\sum_{x=-r}^{r}\hat{\eta}_{s}^{+}(x)
and
1+\sum_{x=-r}^{r}\tau_{s}(x)
are precisely the difference between the label of the first particle to the left of
-r
and the label of the first particle to the right of
r
in
\hat{\eta}_{s}^{+}
and
\tau_{s}
respectively, so we have that
\sum_{x=-r}^{r}\hat{\eta}_{s}^{+}(x)=\sum_{x=-r}^{r}\tau_{s}(x)
under
\mathcal{E}_{1}\cap\mathcal{E}_{2}
.

We can couple
\hat{\eta}_{0}^{-}
with
\tau_{s}
as follows.
Conditioned on
\hat{\eta}_{0}^{-}
, we let
\tau_{s}(x)
for
x=1,2,\ldots,
be i.i.d. Bernoulli
(\rho)
, until some
y^{*}\in\mathbb{Z}
such that
\sum_{x=1}^{y^{*}}\tau_{s}(x)-\hat{\eta}_{0}^{-}(x)=0
, and for any
x>y^{*}
we let
\tau_{s}(x)=\hat{\eta}_{0}^{-}(x)
; we also let

\tau_{s}(x)
for
x=0,-1,\ldots,
be i.i.d. Bernoulli
(\rho)
, until some
y_{*}\in\mathbb{Z}
such that
\sum_{x=y_{*}}^{0}\tau_{s}(x)-\hat{\eta}_{0}^{-}(x)=0
, and for any
x<y_{*}
we let
\tau_{s}(x)=\hat{\eta}_{0}^{-}(x)
.
Let
\mathcal{E}_{*}
be the event where
|y_{*}|,|y^{*}|\leq r
.
Then we have
\mathbb{P}[\mathcal{E}_{*}]>1-Cr^{-1/2}
for some constant
C>0
, since
\hat{\eta}_{0}^{-}
is Bernoulli
(\rho)
on
\mathbb{Z}\setminus\{0,1\}
.
On the other hand, under
\mathcal{E}_{*}
we have
\hat{\eta}_{0}^{-}(x)=\tau_{s}(x)
for any
x\in\mathbb{Z},|x|>r
and
\sum_{x=-r}^{r}\hat{\eta}_{0}^{-}(x)=\sum_{x=-r}^{r}\tau_{s}(x)
.
Thus
\mathcal{E}_{*}\cap\mathcal{E}_{1}\cap\mathcal{E}_{2}
implies
\mathcal{A}
, and
\mathbb{P}[\mathcal{A}]>\mathbb{P}[\mathcal{E}_{1}]+\mathbb{P}[\mathcal{E}_{2}]+\mathbb{P}[\mathcal{E}_{*}]-2
.
Using
\mathbb{P}[\mathcal{E}_{*}]>1-Cr^{-1/2}
and Lemma
6.7
, the conclusion follows.
∎

For the rest of this section we prove Lemma
6.7
.

For any
u\in(\mathbb{Z}^{2}\setminus I_{0}^{\tau})\cup\partial I_{0}^{\tau}
, we also define the ‘semi-infinite geodesic’
\Gamma_{u}^{\tau}
recursively, by letting
\Gamma_{u}^{\tau}[1]=u
, and for each
i\in\mathbb{N}
letting
\Gamma_{u}^{\tau}[i+1]=\argmin_{v\in\{\Gamma_{u}^{\tau}[i]+(1,0),\Gamma_{u}^{\tau}[i]+(0,1)\}}L^{\tau}(v)
.
Note that since
L^{\tau}
is not coupled with the LPP Busemann function, these
\Gamma_{u}^{\tau}
are not actual geodesics.

We consider the following events (see Figure
11
for an illustration of the geometric objects).

\mathcal{E}_{3}:

there exists a vertex
u_{+}\in\partial I_{0}^{+}
, such that
ad(u_{+})<x_{*}
and
\Gamma_{u_{+}}^{+}\cap I_{s}^{+}\subset\mathbb{V}_{(-6r_{3},-r_{1})}
, and
a_{+}^{\prime}>(1-\rho)^{2}s-r_{4}
for
u_{+}^{\prime}=(a_{+}^{\prime},b_{+}^{\prime})
being the last vertex in
\Gamma_{u_{+}}^{+}\cap I_{s}^{+}
.

\mathcal{E}_{3}^{\tau}:

there exists a vertex
u_{\tau}\in\partial I_{0}^{\tau}
such that
ad(u_{\tau})<x_{*}
and
\Gamma_{u_{\tau}}^{\tau}\cap I_{s}^{\tau}\subset\mathbb{V}_{(-6r_{3},-r_{1})}
, and
a_{\tau}^{\prime}>(1-\rho)^{2}s-r_{4}
for
u_{\tau}^{\prime}=(a_{\tau}^{\prime},b_{\tau}^{\prime})
being the last vertex in
\Gamma_{u_{\tau}}^{+}\cap I_{s}^{\tau}
.

\mathcal{E}_{4}:

for each
u=(a,b)\in\partial I_{s}^{+}
with
ad(u)\leq M-r
, we have
a<(1-\rho)^{2}s-r_{4}-1
, and
u+(1,0)\in\mathbb{V}_{(-\infty,-6r_{3})}
.

\mathbf{0}
u_{+}
u_{+}^{\prime}
u_{\tau}
u_{\tau}^{\prime}
I_{0}^{\tau}
I_{0}^{+}
I_{s}^{\tau}
I_{s}^{+}
Q
(a,b):a=(1-\rho)^{2}s-r_{4}
u:ad(u)=M-r
\partial I_{s}^{+}\cap\partial I_{s}^{\tau}
\mathbb{V}_{r_{1}}
\mathbb{V}_{-r_{1}}
\mathbb{V}_{-6r_{3}}

Figure 11
:
The events
\mathcal{E}_{3},\mathcal{E}_{3}^{\tau},\mathcal{E}_{4}
, assuming
p^{P}=p_{s}^{+}
. The shaded region is
Q
in the proof of Lemma
6.8
.

The purpose of these events is as follows. For
\mathcal{E}_{3}
and
\mathcal{E}_{3}^{\tau}
, they ensure that for
u
in a certain region (around
\{u:\mathbb{Z}^{2}:ad(u)\leq M-r\}\cap\partial I_{s}^{+}
), the downward geodesics
\Gamma_{u}^{+,\vee}
and
\Gamma_{u}^{\tau,\vee}
are disjoint from
P
.
Thus using Lemma
6.6
and the coupling between
\xi^{\tau,\vee}
and
\xi^{+,\vee,0}
we have
L^{+}(u)=L^{\tau}(u)
for these
u
.
Then we can deduce that
\{u:\mathbb{Z}^{2}:ad(u)\leq M-r\}\cap\partial I_{s}^{+}
is the same as
\{u:\mathbb{Z}^{2}:ad(u)\leq M-r\}\cap\partial I_{s}^{\tau}
.
Then using
p^{P}=p_{s}^{+}
and Lemma
3.6
, we have that
\mathcal{E}_{1}
holds.
The event
\mathcal{E}_{4}
is to define this ‘certain region’.
In summary, we have the following statement.

Lemma 6.8
.

\{p^{P}=p_{s}^{+}\}\cap\mathcal{E}_{3}\cap\mathcal{E}_{3}^{\tau}\cap\mathcal{E}_{4}\subset\mathcal{E}_{1}
.

Proof.

Below we assume that
\mathcal{E}_{3}\cap\mathcal{E}_{3}^{\tau}\cap\mathcal{E}_{4}
holds and
p^{P}=p_{s}^{+}
.
Denote

Q=\{(a,b)\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}:a<(1-\rho)^{2}s-r_{4},(a,b)\in\mathbb{V}_{(-\infty,-6r_{3})}\}.

See Figure
11
.
Then we must have that
Q\setminus I_{0}^{+}=Q\setminus I_{0}^{\tau}
. Otherwise we can find some
u\in Q
with
u\in\partial I_{0}^{\tau}
and
u\not\in I_{0}^{+}
, or
u\in\partial I_{0}^{+}
and
u\not\in I_{0}^{\tau}
.
In the first case,
u\in u_{\tau}+\mathbb{Z}_{\leq 0}\times\mathbb{Z}_{+}
since
u\in\mathbb{V}_{(-\infty,-6r_{3})}
,
u_{\tau}\in\mathbb{V}_{(-6r_{3},-r_{1})}
, and both
u,u_{\tau}\in\partial I_{0}^{\tau}
. So we must have
ad(u)<ad(u_{\tau})<x_{*}
. But
ad(u)\in\llbracket x_{*},x^{*}\rrbracket
by Lemma
6.6
, so we get a contradiction.
A similar contradiction can be obtained in the second case.

Now take any
u\in Q\setminus I_{0}^{+}=Q\setminus I_{0}^{\tau}
, and we next show that
L^{+}(u)\leq L^{\tau}(u)
.
By Lemma
3.5
we have that
L^{+}(u)=\sum_{v\in\Gamma^{+,\vee}_{u}\setminus I_{0}^{+}}\xi^{+,\vee,0}(v)
, and this is the maximum passage time to
u
from a vertex in
\mathbb{Z}^{2}\setminus I_{0}^{+}
, under the weights
\xi^{+,\vee,0}
.
Analogously,
L^{\tau}(u)
equals the maximum passage time to
u
from a vertex in
\mathbb{Z}^{2}\setminus I_{0}^{\tau}
, under the weights
\xi^{\tau,\vee}
.
It then suffices to show that
\Gamma^{+,\vee}_{u}\setminus I_{0}^{+}
is disjoint from
P
and
I_{0}^{\tau}
, since then
\Gamma^{+,\vee}_{u}\setminus I_{0}^{+}
is an up-right path from a vertex in
\mathbb{Z}^{2}\setminus I_{0}^{\tau}
to
u
, and for any
v\in\Gamma^{+,\vee}_{u}\setminus I_{0}^{+}
we have
\xi^{\tau,\vee}(v)=\xi^{+,\vee,0}(v)
, thus

L^{+}(u)=\sum_{v\in\Gamma^{+,\vee}_{u}\setminus I_{0}^{+}}\xi^{+,\vee,0}(v)=\sum_{v\in\Gamma^{+,\vee}_{u}\setminus I_{0}^{+}}\xi^{\tau,\vee}(v)\leq L^{\tau}(u).

We show that
(\Gamma^{+,\vee}_{u}\setminus I_{0}^{+})\cap(P\cup I_{0}^{\tau})=\emptyset
, using the following steps.

Step 1.

By Lemma
3.3
, the path
\Gamma_{u_{+}}^{+}+(1/2,1/2)
divides
u_{+}+(\mathbb{Z}^{2}\setminus\mathbb{Z}_{\leq 0}^{2})
into two parts, such that
\Gamma^{+,\vee}_{u}
intersects at most one of them.
By
\mathcal{E}_{3}
and
u\in Q\setminus I_{0}^{+}
, we know that
u
must be in the upper-left part, so
\Gamma^{+,\vee}_{u}
cannot intersect the lower-right part.
In particular,
\Gamma^{+,\vee}_{u}
is disjoint from
u_{+}+\mathbb{Z}_{+}\times\mathbb{Z}_{\leq 0}
.
Also
u_{+}+\mathbb{Z}_{\leq 0}^{2}\subset I_{0}^{+}
since
u_{+}\in I_{0}^{+}
, so
\Gamma^{+,\vee}_{u}\setminus I_{0}^{+}\subset u_{+}+\mathbb{Z}\times\mathbb{Z}_{+}
.

Step 2.

If
\Gamma^{+,\vee}_{u}\setminus I_{0}^{+}
is not disjoint from
I_{0}^{\tau}
, take any
v\in(\Gamma^{+,\vee}_{u}\setminus I_{0}^{+})\cap I_{0}^{\tau}
. Then
v\in u_{+}+\mathbb{Z}\times\mathbb{Z}_{+}
according to the previous step. By Lemma
6.6
we have
ad(v)\geq x_{*}
, and
\mathcal{E}_{3}
states that
ad(u_{+})<x_{*}
. So
ad(v)>ad(u_{+})
, thus
v-u_{+}\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}^{2}
. But this implies that
u_{+}+(1,1)\in I_{0}^{\tau}\setminus I_{0}^{+}
, which contradicts with Lemma
6.6
since
ad(u_{+}+(1,1))=ad(u_{+})<x_{*}
.

Step 3.

Since
u_{+}\subset\mathbb{V}_{(-6r_{3},-r_{1})}
(by
\mathcal{E}_{3}
), we have
P\cap(u_{+}+\mathbb{Z}_{\leq 0}\times\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0})=\emptyset
. We also have that
u_{+}+\mathbb{Z}_{\leq 0}^{2}\subset I_{0}^{+}
since
u_{+}\in I_{0}^{+}
. Thus we have
P\setminus I_{0}^{+}\subset u_{+}+\mathbb{Z}_{+}\times\mathbb{Z}
.
Take any
(a,b)\in P\setminus I_{0}^{+}
.
If
a\geq(1-\rho)^{2}s-r_{4}
, we cannot have
(a,b)\in\Gamma^{+,\vee}_{u}
since
u\in Q
.
If
a<(1-\rho)^{2}s-r_{4}
, by
\mathcal{E}_{3}
we must have that
(a,b)
is in the lower-right part from Step 1, so still we must have that
(a,b)\not\in\Gamma^{+,\vee}_{u}
.
So
P\setminus I_{0}^{+}
is disjoint from
\Gamma^{+,\vee}_{u}
, and equivalently
\Gamma^{+,\vee}_{u}\setminus I_{0}^{+}
is disjoint from
P
.

So far we have shown
L^{+}(u)\leq L^{\tau}(u)
. We can also show
L^{+}(u)\leq L^{\tau}(u)
with essentially verbatim arguments, using
\mathcal{E}_{3}^{\tau}
instead of
\mathcal{E}_{3}
.
We then conclude that
L^{+}(u)=L^{\tau}(u)
for any
u\in Q\setminus I_{0}^{+}=Q\setminus I_{0}^{\tau}
.

We then show that
\mathcal{E}_{1}
holds, using Lemma
3.6
.
Specifically, take any
x\in\mathbb{Z}
with
x<-r
, we next show that
\hat{\eta}_{s}^{+}(x)=\tau(x)
, and the particles (or holes) have the same labels.

We first assume that
\hat{\eta}_{s}^{+}(x)=1
.
Then
{\eta_{s}^{+}}(x+ad(p_{s}^{+}))=1
.
By
p^{P}=p_{s}^{+}
we have
M=ad(p_{s}^{+})
, so
{\eta_{s}^{+}}(x+M)=1
.
By Lemma
3.6
, there is some
y\in\mathbb{Z}
, such that
(M+x+y-1,y)\in I_{s}^{+}
and
(M+x+y,y)\not\in I_{s}^{+}
, and the particle at
x
in
\hat{\eta}_{s}^{+}
has label
y
.
Since
ad(M+x+y-1,y)=M+x-1\leq M-r
and
(M+x+y-1,y)\in\partial I_{s}^{+}
, by
\mathcal{E}_{4}
we have
M+x+y<(1-\rho)^{2}s-r_{4}
and
(M+x+y,y)\in\mathbb{V}_{(-\infty,-6r_{3})}
.
Thus
(M+x+y,y),(M+x+y-1,y)\in Q
, and

L^{\tau}(M+x+y,y)=L^{+}(M+x+y,y)>s\geq L^{+}(M+x+y-1,y)=L^{\tau}(M+x+y-1,y).

This implies that
(M+x+y-1,y)\in I_{s}^{\tau}
and
(M+x+y,y)\not\in I_{s}^{\tau}
.
Then by (an analogue of) Lemma
3.6
we have
\tau_{s}(x)=1
, and the particle at
x
in
\tau_{s}
has label
y
.

Similarly, if we assume that
\hat{\eta}_{s}^{+}(x)=0
, we can deduce that
\tau_{s}(x)=0
, and the holes have the same label.
By taking
x
over all integers
<-r
, we conclude that
\mathcal{E}_{1}
holds assuming
p^{P}=p_{s}^{+}
and
\mathcal{E}_{3}\cap\mathcal{E}_{4}\cap\mathcal{E}_{3}^{\tau}
.
∎

It now suffices to lower bound the probabilities of the events
\mathcal{E}_{3}
,
\mathcal{E}_{3}^{\tau}
,
\mathcal{E}_{4}
.

Lemma 6.9
.

\mathbb{P}[\mathcal{E}_{3}],\mathbb{P}[\mathcal{E}_{3}^{\tau}]>1-C\alpha^{-1/2}
for constants
c,C>0
.

Lemma 6.10
.

\mathbb{P}[\mathcal{E}_{4}]>1-Ce^{-c\alpha}
for constants
c,C>0
.

Using Lemma
6.8
and Lemmas
6.9
,
6.10
, we get lower bound for
\mathbb{P}[\mathcal{E}_{1}]
. We can lower bound
\mathbb{P}[\mathcal{E}_{2}]
similarly. Thus Lemma
6.7
follows.

To prove these estimates we introduce some other setups.
For the convenience of notations, we extend
\xi^{+,\vee,0}
from
\mathbb{Z}^{2}\setminus I_{0}^{+}
to
\mathbb{Z}^{2}
, so that conditioned on
I_{0}^{+}
,
\{\xi^{+,\vee,0}(v)\}_{v\in I_{0}^{+}}
are i.i.d.
\Exp(1)
and are independent of everything else.
For each
u\leq v
, we let
T^{+,\vee,0}_{u,v}
and
\Gamma^{+,\vee,0}_{u,v}
be the passage time and geodesic from
u
to
v
under the weights
\xi^{+,\vee,0}
.
For any
v\not\in I_{0}^{+}
we denote
\Gamma^{+,\vee,0}_{I,v}=\Gamma^{+,\vee,0}_{u_{*},v}
and
T^{+,\vee,0}_{I,v}=T^{+,\vee,0}_{u_{*},v}
, where
u_{*}=\argmax_{u\leq v,u\not\in I_{0}^{+}}T^{+,\vee,0}_{u,v}
.
In words,
\Gamma^{+,\vee,0}_{I,v}
and
T^{+,\vee,0}_{I,v}
are the geodesic and passage time from boundary
I_{0}^{+}
to
v
, under the weights
\xi^{+,\vee,0}
. By Lemma
3.5
we have
\Gamma^{+,\vee,0}_{I,v}=\Gamma^{+,\vee}_{v}\setminus I_{0}^{+}
and
T^{+,\vee,0}_{I,v}=L^{+}(v)
.

Proof of Lemma
6.9
.

We shall write the proof for the estimates of
\mathbb{P}[\mathcal{E}_{3}]
, and the approach we take here applies to
\mathbb{P}[\mathcal{E}_{3}^{\tau}]
essentially verbatim.
We will use
c,C>0
to denote small and large enough constants, and their values can change from line to line.

We consider the following events (see Figure
12
).

\mathcal{E}_{5}:

x_{*}>-r_{3}
.

\mathcal{E}_{6}:

\mathbb{V}_{(-jr_{4},jr_{4})}\cap\partial I_{0}^{+}\subset\mathbb{H}_{(-j\alpha r_{4}^{1/2},j\alpha r_{4}^{1/2})}
for each

.

\mathcal{E}_{7}:

\mathbb{V}_{(-6r_{3},-r_{3})}\cap\partial I_{s}^{+}\subset\mathbb{H}_{(s-2\alpha r_{4}^{1/2},s+2\alpha r_{4}^{1/2})}
.

\mathcal{E}_{8}:

Let

with

and

be the intersection of
\mathbb{H}_{2s}
with
\mathbb{V}_{-2r_{3}}
(rounded to the nearest lattice vertex).
Then
\Gamma_{I,u_{1}}^{+,\vee,0}\subset\mathbb{V}_{(-6r_{3},-4r_{3})}
and
\Gamma_{I,u_{2}}^{+,\vee,0}\subset\mathbb{V}_{(-3r_{3},-r_{3})}
.

The events
\mathcal{E}_{6}
and
\mathcal{E}_{7}
just say that
\partial I_{0}^{+}
and
\partial I_{s}^{+}
behave ‘typically’ in certain regions. The event
\mathcal{E}_{8}
is to bound the transversal fluctuation of
\Gamma_{u_{+}}^{+}
(for some
u_{+}\in\partial I_{0}^{+}
), using the non-crossing property of downward and upward semi-infinite geodesics (Lemma
3.3
).

u_{1}
u_{2}
\mathbf{0}
u_{+}
u_{+}^{\prime}
\partial I_{0}^{+}
\partial I_{s}^{+}
\mathbb{V}_{r_{3}}
\mathbb{V}_{-r_{3}}
\mathbb{V}_{-3r_{3}}
\mathbb{V}_{-4r_{3}}
\mathbb{V}_{-6r_{3}}
\Gamma_{u_{+}}^{+}
\Gamma_{I,u_{1}}^{+,\vee,0}
\Gamma_{I,u_{2}}^{+,\vee,0}
\mathbb{V}_{(-6r_{3},-r_{3})}\cap\mathbb{H}_{(s-2\alpha r_{4}^{1/2},s+2\alpha r_{4}^{1/2})}
S_{*}\setminus S^{*}

Figure 12
:
The events to lower bound
\mathbb{P}[\mathcal{E}_{3}]
.

We next show that
\mathcal{E}_{5}\cap\mathcal{E}_{6}\cap\mathcal{E}_{7}\cap\mathcal{E}_{8}\subset\mathcal{E}_{3}
.
For this, we take any
u_{+}\in\partial I_{0}^{+}\cap\mathbb{V}_{(-4r_{3},-3r_{3})}
, and let
u_{+}^{\prime}=(a_{+}^{\prime},b_{+}^{\prime})
be the last vertex in
\Gamma_{u_{+}}^{+}\cap I_{s}^{+}
.
Then we need to show that
ad(u_{+})<x_{*}
,
\Gamma_{u_{+}}^{+}\cap I_{s}^{+}\subset\mathbb{V}_{(-6r_{3},-r_{3})}
, and
a_{+}^{\prime}>(1-\rho)^{2}s-r_{4}
, assuming
\mathcal{E}_{5}\cap\mathcal{E}_{6}\cap\mathcal{E}_{7}\cap\mathcal{E}_{8}
.

•

By
\mathcal{E}_{6}
, and note that
r_{3}>C\alpha r_{4}^{1/2}
by our choice of the parameters, we have
ad(u_{+})<-r_{3}
. So under
\mathcal{E}_{5}\cap\mathcal{E}_{6}
we have
ad(u_{+})<x_{*}
.

•

Under
\mathcal{E}_{8}
, the path
\Gamma_{I,u_{1}}^{+,\vee,0}-(1/2,1/2)
divides
(u_{1}+(\mathbb{Z}^{2}\setminus\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}^{2}))\setminus I_{s}^{+}
into two parts:
(\cup_{v\in\Gamma_{I,u_{1}}^{+,\vee,0}}v+\mathbb{Z}_{-}\times\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0})\setminus I_{s}^{+}
and
(\cup_{v\in\Gamma_{I,u_{1}}^{+,\vee,0}}v+\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}\times\mathbb{Z}_{-})\setminus I_{s}^{+}
.
Then
u_{+}
must be in the second part, so by Lemma
3.3

\Gamma_{u_{+}}^{+}
must be disjoint from the first part, thus
\Gamma_{u_{+}}^{+}\cap\mathbb{V}_{(-\infty,-6r_{3}]}\subset u_{1}+\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}^{2}
.
If
\Gamma_{u_{+}}^{+}\cap\mathbb{V}_{(-\infty,-6r_{3}]}\cap I_{s}^{+}
is not empty, we must have
u_{1}\in I_{s}^{+}
, which contradicts
\mathcal{E}_{7}
.
So under
\mathcal{E}_{7}\cap\mathcal{E}_{8}
we must have that
\Gamma_{u_{+}}^{+}\cap I_{s}^{+}
is disjoint from
\mathbb{V}_{(-\infty,-6r_{3}]}
, and similarly it is also disjoint from
\mathbb{V}_{[-r_{3},\infty)}
. These mean that
\Gamma_{u_{+}}^{+}\cap I_{s}^{+}\subset\mathbb{V}_{(-6r_{3},-r_{3})}
.

•

\{\Gamma_{u_{+}}^{+}\cap I_{s}^{+}\subset\mathbb{V}_{(-6r_{3},-r_{3})}\}\cap\mathcal{E}_{7}
implies that
u_{+}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{H}_{(s-2\alpha r_{4}^{1/2},s+2\alpha r_{4}^{1/2})}\cap\mathbb{V}_{(-6r_{3},-r_{3})}
.
Thus we get
a_{+}^{\prime}>(1-\rho)^{2}s-r_{4}
since
r_{4}>Cr_{3},C\alpha r_{4}^{1/2}
by our choice of the parameters.

It remains to estimate the probabilities of these events and take a union bound.

Bound
\mathbb{P}[\mathcal{E}_{5}]
.
By the coupling between
\tau_{0}
and
({\eta_{t}^{+}})_{t\geq 0}
(stated after the proof of Lemma
6.5
), the number
-x_{*}
is just the time of a symmetric random walk hitting
0
after
r_{2}
. Thus
\mathbb{P}[\mathcal{E}_{5}]\geq 1-Cr_{2}^{1/2}r_{3}^{-1/2}=1-C\alpha^{-1/2}
.

Bound
\mathbb{P}[\mathcal{E}_{6}]
.
The event
\mathcal{E}_{6}
is again on the hitting probability of a random walk.
Indeed, by Lemma
3.6
, if we let
f(x)
be the largest integer with
(f(x)+x,f(x))\in I_{0}^{+}
, we must have
f(0)=0
,
f(x)=\sum_{y=1}^{x}-\eta_{0}^{+}(x)
for any
x\geq 1
, and
f(x)=\sum_{y=x+1}^{0}\eta_{0}^{+}(x)
for any
x\leq-1
;
and
\{\eta_{0}^{+}(x)\}_{x\in\mathbb{Z}\setminus\{0,1\}}
are i.i.d. Bernoulli
(\rho)
.
Thus for each
j\in\mathbb{N}
we have
\mathbb{P}[\mathbb{V}_{(-jr_{4},jr_{4})}\cap\partial I_{0}^{+}\subset\mathbb{H}_{(-j\alpha r_{4}^{1/2},j\alpha r_{4}^{1/2})}]>1-Ce^{-cj\alpha^{2}}
, so when
\alpha>C
we have
\mathbb{P}[\mathcal{E}_{6}]\geq 1-Ce^{-c\alpha^{2}}
.

For
\mathbb{P}[\mathcal{E}_{7}]
and
\mathbb{P}[\mathcal{E}_{8}]
, we reduce them to estimates on last-passage times and geodesic transversal fluctuations under the weights
\xi^{+,\vee,0}
, and use results from Section
5
.

Bound
\mathbb{P}[\mathcal{E}_{7}]
.

We note that
\mathcal{E}_{7}
is implied by the following two events:

•

T^{+,\vee,0}_{I,v}=L^{+}(v)>s
whenever
v\in\mathbb{V}_{(-6r_{3},-r_{3})}\cap\mathbb{H}_{[s+2\alpha r_{4}^{1/2},\infty)}\cap\mathbb{Z}^{2}
,

•

T^{+,\vee,0}_{I,v}=L^{+}(v)\leq s
whenever
v-(1,1)\in\mathbb{V}_{(-6r_{3},-r_{3})}\cap\mathbb{H}_{(-\infty,s-2\alpha r_{4}^{1/2}]}\cap\mathbb{Z}^{2}
.

These two events imply that
\mathbb{V}_{(-6r_{3},-r_{3})}\cap\mathbb{H}_{(-\infty,s-2\alpha r_{4}^{1/2}]\cup[s+2\alpha r_{4}^{1/2},\infty)}
is disjoint from
\partial I_{s}^{+}
, so
\mathcal{E}_{7}
holds.

To estimate the probabilities of these events, we need to bound the passage times under
\xi^{+,\vee,0}
.
For this, we set up the following notations.
For each
j\in\mathbb{N}
we let

S_{j}=\mathbb{V}_{(-jr_{4},-(j-1)r_{4}]\cup[(j-1)r_{4},jr_{4})}\cap\mathbb{H}_{(-j\alpha r_{4}^{1/2},\infty)},

S^{j}=\mathbb{V}_{(-jr_{4},-(j-1)r_{4}]\cup[(j-1)r_{4},jr_{4})}\cap\mathbb{H}_{[j\alpha r_{4}^{1/2},\infty)}.

Let
S_{*}=\cup_{j\in\mathbb{N}}S_{j}
and
S^{*}=\cup_{j\in\mathbb{N}}S^{j}
.
Then the event
\mathcal{E}_{6}
precisely says that
\partial I_{0}^{+}\subset S_{*}\setminus S^{*}
(see Figure
12
), and implies that
S^{*}\subset\mathbb{Z}^{2}\setminus I_{0}^{+}\subset S_{*}
.

We consider the following events:

\mathcal{E}_{7}^{\prime}:

T^{+,\vee,0}_{u,v}\leq s
for any vertices
u\in S_{*}
and
v\in\mathbb{V}_{(-6r_{3},-r_{3})}\cap\mathbb{H}_{(-\infty,s-2\alpha r_{4}^{1/2}]}
with
u\leq v
.

\mathcal{E}_{7}^{\prime\prime}:

For any
v\in\mathbb{V}_{(-6r_{3},-r_{3})}\cap\mathbb{H}_{[s+2\alpha r_{4}^{1/2},\infty)}\cap\mathbb{Z}^{2}
, there exists
u\in\mathbb{V}_{(-6r_{3},-r_{3})}\cap\mathbb{H}_{(\alpha r_{4}^{1/2},\infty)}\cap\mathbb{Z}^{2}
such that
T^{+,\vee,0}_{u,v}>s
.

Then under
\mathcal{E}_{7}^{\prime}\cap\mathcal{E}_{7}^{\prime\prime}\cap\mathcal{E}_{6}
, the two events above hold, thus
\mathcal{E}_{7}
holds.

\mathbf{0}

(a)

\mathbb{P}[\mathcal{E}_{7}]
: assuming
\mathcal{E}_{6}
, the event
\mathcal{E}_{7}
is implied by
\mathcal{E}_{7}^{\prime}\cap\mathcal{E}_{7}^{\prime\prime}
, about passage times under the weights
\xi^{+,\vee,0}
. For

, we need to upper bound the passage times from

to

; for

, we need to lower bound the passage times from around

to

(the blue segments).

(b)

\mathbb{P}[\mathcal{E}_{8}]
: under
\mathcal{E}_{8}^{\prime}\cap\mathcal{E}_{6}
, we must have that
u_{3}
(the lower endpoint of
\Gamma_{I,u_{1}}^{+,\vee,0}
) is in
\mathbb{V}_{(-5r_{3}-r_{2},-5r_{3}+r_{2})}
. Then if
\Gamma^{+,\vee,0}_{u_{4},u_{1}}
and
\Gamma^{+,\vee,0}_{u_{5},u_{1}}
below
\mathbb{H}_{2\alpha r_{4}^{1/2}}
are contained in
\mathbb{V}_{(-5r_{3}-3r_{2},-5r_{3}-r_{2})}
and
\mathbb{V}_{(-5r_{3}+r_{2},-5r_{3}+3r_{2})}
, respectively,
\Gamma_{I,u_{1}}^{+,\vee,0}
is sandwiched between them, and the transversal fluctuation of
\Gamma_{I,u_{1}}^{+,\vee,0}
is controlled by
\Gamma^{+,\vee,0}_{u_{4},u_{1}}
and
\Gamma^{+,\vee,0}_{u_{5},u_{1}}
.

Figure 13
:
Illustrations of bounding
\mathbb{P}[\mathcal{E}_{7}]
and

in the proof of Lemma
6.9
.

We next lower bound the probabilities
\mathbb{P}[\mathcal{E}_{7}^{\prime}]
and
\mathbb{P}[\mathcal{E}_{7}^{\prime\prime}]
. These bounds are deduced from the estimates of Theorem
5.2
and Proposition
5.3
.

We first consider
\mathbb{P}[\mathcal{E}_{7}^{\prime}]
. For each
j\in\mathbb{N}
, we let
P_{j}
be the collection of all vertices in
\mathbb{Z}^{2}
that are within distance
1
from
\mathbb{V}_{(-jr_{4},-(j-1)r_{4}]\cup[(j-1)r_{4},jr_{4})}\cap\mathbb{H}_{-j\alpha r_{4}^{1/2}}
, and let
P_{*}
be the collection of all vertices in
\mathbb{Z}^{2}
that are within distance
1
from
\mathbb{V}_{(-6r_{3},-r_{3})}\cap\mathbb{H}_{s-2\alpha r_{4}^{1/2}}
.
To lower bound
\mathbb{P}[\mathcal{E}_{7}^{\prime}]
, we just need to consider
T^{+,\vee,0}_{u,v}
, for all
u\in\cup_{j\in\mathbb{N}}P_{j}
and
v\in P_{*}
(see Figure
13(a)
).
We note that for any
j\in\mathbb{N}
and any
u\in P_{j}
,
v\in P_{*}
with
u\leq v
, if we write
(a,b)=v-u
we have

(\sqrt{a}+\sqrt{b})^{2}<s-c\alpha r_{4}^{1/2}-c(j-1)^{2}r_{4}^{2}s^{-1}.

(6.9)

For
j>csr_{4}^{-1}+1
, we apply (
5.1
) in Theorem
5.2
to each
u\in P_{j}
and
v\in P_{*}
and take a union bound, to conclude that

\mathbb{P}[T^{+,\vee,0}_{u,v}\leq s,\;\forall u\in P_{j},v\in P_{*},u\leq v,j>csr_{4}^{-1}+1]>1-Csr_{3}e^{-c\sqrt{s}}.

For any
j\leq csr_{4}^{-1}+1
, the slope of
v-u
for any
u\in P_{j}
and
v\in P_{*}
is bounded away from
0
and
\infty
.
Thus we can split
P_{j}
and
P_{*}
into
Cr_{4}s^{-2/3}
and
Cr_{3}s^{-2/3}
segments of length
<Cs^{2/3}
, and apply Proposition
5.3
.
Note that using (
5.3
) from Theorem
5.2
and (
6.9
), we have that
\mathbb{E}[T^{+,\vee,0}_{u,v}]<s-cj^{2}\alpha^{3}s^{1/3}
for any
u\in P_{j}
and
v\in P_{*}
.
We then conclude that

\mathbb{P}[T^{+,\vee,0}_{u,v}\leq s,\;\forall u\in P_{j},v\in P_{*}]>1-C(r_{3}s^{-2/3})(r_{4}s^{-2/3})e^{-cj^{2}\alpha^{3}}.

Thus we have that

\mathbb{P}[\mathcal{E}_{7}^{\prime}]>1-Csr_{3}e^{-c\sqrt{s}}-Cr_{3}r_{4}s^{-4/3}\sum_{j\in\mathbb{N}}e^{-cj^{2}\alpha^{3}}=1-Cs^{5/3}\alpha^{3}e^{-c\sqrt{s}}-C\alpha^{7}\sum_{j\in\mathbb{N}}e^{-cj^{2}\alpha^{3}}.

For
\mathbb{P}[\mathcal{E}_{7}^{\prime\prime}]
, we need to consider
T^{+,\vee,0}_{u,v}
, for all
v\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}\cap\mathbb{V}_{(-6r_{3},-r_{3})}
within distance
1
from
\mathbb{H}_{s+2\alpha r_{4}^{1/2}}
, and
u\in\mathbb{V}_{(-6r_{3},-r_{3})}\cap\mathbb{H}_{(\alpha r_{4}^{1/2},\infty)}\cap\mathbb{Z}^{2}
within distance
1
from
v-(s+\alpha r_{4}^{1/2}/2)\boldsymbol{\rho}
(see Figure
13(a)
).
For such
u
and
v
, the slope of
v-u
is bounded away from
0
and
\infty
.
By (
5.3
) we have
\mathbb{E}[T^{+,\vee,0}_{u,v}]>s+c\alpha^{3}s^{1/3}
.
We then apply Proposition
5.3
by covering all such
u,v
with
Cr_{3}s^{-2/3}
parallelograms of size
Cs\times Cs^{2/3}
, and we conclude that
\mathbb{P}[\mathcal{E}_{7}^{\prime\prime}]>1-Cr_{3}s^{-2/3}e^{-c\alpha^{3}}=1-C\alpha^{3}e^{-c\alpha^{3}}
.

In summary and using the fact that
Cs^{2/3}<r<s^{2/3+0.01}
from the statement of Lemma
6.2
(thus
\alpha>C
and
\alpha<s^{0.002}
), we have
\mathbb{P}[\mathcal{E}_{6}\setminus\mathcal{E}_{7}]<Ce^{-c\alpha^{3}}
.

Bound
\mathbb{P}[\mathcal{E}_{8}]
.
We denote
u_{3}
as the lower endpoint of
\Gamma_{I,u_{1}}^{+,\vee,0}
.
Consider the event
\mathcal{E}_{8}^{\prime}
, where

•

for any
u\in(S_{*}\setminus\mathbb{V}_{(-5r_{3}-r_{2},-5r_{3}+r_{2})})\cap\mathbb{Z}^{2}
, there is
T^{+,\vee,0}_{u,u_{1}}<2s-4\alpha r_{4}^{1/2}
,

•

T^{+,\vee,0}_{u_{1}^{\prime},u_{1}}>2s-4\alpha r_{4}^{1/2}
, where
u_{1}^{\prime}
is the intersection of
\mathbb{H}_{2\alpha r_{4}^{1/2}}
with
\mathbb{V}_{-5r_{3}}
(rounded to the nearest lattice vertex). Note that
u_{1}^{\prime}\in S^{*}\cap\mathbb{V}_{(-5r_{3}-r_{2},-5r_{3}+r_{2})}
.

Under
\mathcal{E}_{8}^{\prime}\cap\mathcal{E}_{6}
we must have
u_{3}\in\mathbb{V}_{(-5r_{3}-r_{2},-5r_{3}+r_{2})}
, since
T_{u_{3},u_{1}}^{+,\vee,0}
is the maximum passage time from
I_{0}^{+}
to
u_{1}
(see Figure
13(b)
).
We can deduce that
\mathbb{P}[\mathcal{E}_{8}^{\prime}]>1-Ce^{-c\alpha^{3}}
similar to how
\mathbb{P}[\mathcal{E}_{7}^{\prime}]
and
\mathbb{P}[\mathcal{E}_{7}^{\prime\prime}]
are bounded above using Theorem
5.2
and Proposition
5.3
, and we omit the details.

Now we take
u_{4},u_{5}
as the intersection of
\mathbb{H}_{-\alpha r_{4}^{1/2}}
with
\mathbb{V}_{-5r_{3}-2r_{2}}
and
\mathbb{V}_{-5r_{3}+2r_{2}}
, respectively (rounded to the nearest lattice vertex, see Figure
13(b)
).
Consider
\Gamma^{+,\vee,0}_{u_{4},u_{1}}
and
\Gamma^{+,\vee,0}_{u_{5},u_{1}}
.
By Corollary
5.9
we have

\mathbb{P}[\Gamma^{+,\vee,0}_{u_{4},u_{1}}\cap\mathbb{H}_{(-\alpha r_{4}^{1/2},2\alpha r_{4}^{1/2})}\subset\mathbb{V}_{(-5r_{3}-3r_{2},-5r_{3}-r_{2})}]>1-Ce^{-cr_{2}^{3}\alpha^{-2}r_{4}^{-1}},

(6.10)

\mathbb{P}[\Gamma^{+,\vee,0}_{u_{5},u_{1}}\cap\mathbb{H}_{(-\alpha r_{4}^{1/2},2\alpha r_{4}^{1/2})}\subset\mathbb{V}_{(-5r_{3}+r_{2},-5r_{3}+3r_{2})}]>1-Ce^{-cr_{2}^{3}\alpha^{-2}r_{4}^{-1}},

(6.11)

and by Lemma
5.7
we have

\mathbb{P}[\Gamma^{+,\vee,0}_{u_{4},u_{1}}\subset\mathbb{V}_{(-6r_{3},-4r_{3})}],\;\mathbb{P}[\Gamma^{+,\vee,0}_{u_{5},u_{1}}\subset\mathbb{V}_{(-6r_{3},-4r_{3})}]>1-Ce^{-cr_{3}^{3}s^{-2}}.

(6.12)

When
\mathcal{E}_{8}^{\prime}\cap\mathcal{E}_{6}
happens, we have
u_{3}\in\mathbb{V}_{(-5r_{3}-r_{2},-5r_{3}+r_{2})}\cap\mathbb{H}_{(-\alpha r_{4}^{1/2},2\alpha r_{4}^{1/2})}
.
If the events in the left-hand side of (
6.10
) and (
6.11
) also happen,
we must have that
\Gamma_{I,u_{1}}^{+,\vee,0}=\Gamma^{+,\vee,0}_{u_{3},u_{1}}
is between
\Gamma^{+,\vee,0}_{u_{4},u_{1}}
and
\Gamma^{+,\vee,0}_{u_{5},u_{1}}
, by ordering of geodesics (Lemma
5.1
).
If in addition the event in the left-hand side of (
6.12
) happens, we have

\Gamma_{I,u_{1}}^{+,\vee,0}=\Gamma^{+,\vee,0}_{u_{3},u_{1}}\subset\mathbb{V}_{(-6r_{3},-4r_{3})}
.
We can use similar arguments to study the event
\Gamma_{I,u_{2}}^{+,\vee,0}\subset\mathbb{V}_{(-3r_{3},-r_{3})}
.
Then with
\mathbb{P}[\mathcal{E}_{8}^{\prime}]>1-Ce^{-c\alpha^{3}}
we conclude that
\mathbb{P}[\mathcal{E}_{6}\setminus\mathcal{E}_{8}]<Ce^{-c\alpha^{3}}+Ce^{-cr_{2}^{3}\alpha^{-2}r_{4}^{-1}}+Ce^{-cr_{3}^{3}s^{-2}}=Ce^{-c\alpha^{3}}+Ce^{-cs^{4/3}}+Ce^{-c\alpha^{9}}
.
Using
Cs^{2/3}<r<s^{2/3+0.01}
(from the statement of Lemma
6.2
), this is bounded by
Ce^{-c\alpha^{3}}
.

Putting together the bounds for
\mathbb{P}[\mathcal{E}_{5}],\mathbb{P}[\mathcal{E}_{6}],\mathbb{P}[\mathcal{E}_{6}\setminus\mathcal{E}_{7}],\mathbb{P}[\mathcal{E}_{6}\setminus\mathcal{E}_{8}]
we conclude that
\mathbb{P}[\mathcal{E}_{3}]>1-C\alpha^{-1/2}
.
∎

Proof of Lemma
6.10
.

We again use
c,C>0
to denote small and large enough constants, and their values can change from line to line.
We consider three events.

\mathcal{E}_{9}:

|M-(1-2\rho)s|<r_{2}
.

\mathcal{E}_{10}:

for any
u=(a,b)
with
ad(u)=a-b<(1-2\rho)s+r_{2}-r
and
a\geq(1-\rho)^{2}s-r_{4}-1
, we have
u\not\in I_{s}^{+}
.

\mathcal{E}_{11}:

for any
u=(a,b)
with
a<(1-\rho)^{2}s-r_{4}
and
u-(0,1)\in\mathbb{V}_{[-6r_{3},\infty)}
, we have
u\in I_{s}^{+}
.

We have that
\mathcal{E}_{9}\cap\mathcal{E}_{10}\cap\mathcal{E}_{11}\subset\mathcal{E}_{4}
.
Indeed,
\mathcal{E}_{9}\cap\mathcal{E}_{10}
implies that
\partial I_{s}^{+}
is disjoint from

\{(a,b)\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}:\;a-b\leq M-r,\;a\geq(1-\rho)^{2}s-r_{4}-1\};

and
\mathcal{E}_{11}
implies that
\partial I_{s}^{+}
is disjoint from

\{(a,b)\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}:\;(a+1,b)\in\mathbb{V}_{[-6r_{3},\infty)},\;a<(1-\rho)^{2}s-r_{4}-1\},

since this set shifted by
(1,1)
is contained in
I_{s}^{+}
by
\mathcal{E}_{11}
.
See Figure
11
for an illustration of these regions.
Thus under
\mathcal{E}_{9}\cap\mathcal{E}_{10}\cap\mathcal{E}_{11}
, for any
u=(a,b)\in\partial I_{s}^{+}
with
ad(u)\leq M-r
we must have
a<(1-\rho)^{2}s-r_{4}-1
and
u+(1,0)\in\mathbb{V}_{(-\infty,-6r_{3})}
.
So we conclude that
\mathcal{E}_{9}\cap\mathcal{E}_{10}\cap\mathcal{E}_{11}\subset\mathcal{E}_{4}
, and it remains to lower bound
\mathbb{P}[\mathcal{E}_{9}]
,
\mathbb{P}[\mathcal{E}_{10}]
, and
\mathbb{P}[\mathcal{E}_{11}]
.

Bound
\mathbb{P}[\mathcal{E}_{9}]
.

By Lemma
3.1
,
p_{s}^{+}-(1,0)
or
p_{s}^{+}-(0,1)
is the last vertex in
\{u\in\Gamma_{\mathbf{0}}^{+}:T_{\mathbf{0},u}^{+}\leq s\}
since
p_{s}^{+}
is the last vertex in
\{u\in\Gamma_{\mathbf{0}}^{+}:\mathbf{G}^{+}(u)\leq s\}
.
So by Corollary
5.11
we have
\mathbb{P}[|ad(p_{s}^{+})-(1-2\rho)s|<r_{2}]>1-Ce^{-cr_{2}^{3}s^{-2}}
.
Then by Lemma
6.5
we have that
\mathbb{P}[\mathcal{E}_{9}]>1-Ce^{-cr_{2}^{3}s^{-2}}-Ce^{-c\alpha^{3}}=1-Ce^{-c\alpha^{6}}-Ce^{-c\alpha^{3}}
.

To bound
\mathbb{P}[\mathcal{E}_{10}]
and
\mathbb{P}[\mathcal{E}_{11}]
, we just need to bound the function
L^{+}
at certain vertices.
For this, we recall the event
\mathcal{E}_{6}
and sets
S_{*}
,
S^{*}
from the proof of Lemma
6.9
.

Bound
\mathbb{P}[\mathcal{E}_{10}]
.

We take
u^{*}=(a^{*},b^{*})
where
a^{*}=\lceil(1-\rho)^{2}s-r_{4}-1\rceil
and
b^{*}=a^{*}-\lceil(1-2\rho)s+r_{2}-r\rceil
, then
\mathcal{E}_{10}
is equivalent to
L^{+}(u^{*})=T^{+,\vee,0}_{I,u^{*}}>s
.
Denote
{u^{*}_{-}}=(\lfloor r_{4}\rfloor,\lfloor r_{4}\rfloor)
. As
u^{*}_{-}\in S^{*}
, under
\mathcal{E}_{6}
we have
u_{-}^{*}\not\in I_{0}^{+}
.
Thus under
\mathcal{E}_{6}\setminus\mathcal{E}_{10}
we have

T^{+,\vee,0}_{{u^{*}_{-}},u^{*}}\leq s
. Then
\mathbb{P}[\mathcal{E}_{6}\setminus\mathcal{E}_{10}]\leq\mathbb{P}[T^{+,\vee,0}_{{u^{*}_{-}},u^{*}}\leq s]<Ce^{-cr^{3}/s}
, where the last inequality is by the fact that
(\sqrt{a_{*}-\lfloor r_{4}\rfloor}+\sqrt{b_{*}-\lfloor r_{4}\rfloor})^{2}>s+cr
and (
5.2
) in Theorem
5.2
.

Bound
\mathbb{P}[\mathcal{E}_{11}]
.

Let
u_{*}=(a_{*},b_{*})
where
a_{*}=\lceil(1-\rho)^{2}s-r_{4}-1\rceil
, and
b_{*}
is the largest integer such that
u_{*}-(0,1)\in\mathbb{V}_{[-6r_{3},\infty)}
. Then
\mathcal{E}_{11}
is equivalent to that
L^{+}(u)=T^{+,v,0}_{I,u_{*}}\leq s
.
Under
\mathcal{E}_{6}\setminus\mathcal{E}_{11}
we have that there is some
u\in S_{*}
,
u\leq u_{*}
, such that
T^{+,v,0}_{u,u_{*}}>s
.
We note that for any
u\in S_{*}
with
u\leq u_{*}
, if we let
(a,b)=u_{*}-u
, there is
(\sqrt{a}+\sqrt{b})^{2}<s-cr_{4}
.
Then by (
5.1
) in Theorem
5.2
and a union bound (over all
u\in S_{*}
such that
u\in u_{*}+\mathbb{Z}_{\leq 0}^{2}
and
u-(1,1)\not\in S_{*}
), we have
\mathbb{P}[\mathcal{E}_{6}\setminus\mathcal{E}_{11}]<Cse^{-cr_{4}s^{-1/2}}
.

Putting together the bounds for
\mathbb{P}[\mathcal{E}_{9}],\mathbb{P}[\mathcal{E}_{6}\setminus\mathcal{E}_{10}],\mathbb{P}[\mathcal{E}_{6}\setminus\mathcal{E}_{11}]
and the bound for
\mathbb{P}[\mathcal{E}_{6}]
in the proof of Lemma
6.9
, and using the fact that
Cs^{2/3}<r<s^{2/3+0.01}
from the statement of Lemma
6.2
(thus
\alpha>C
and
Ce^{-cr^{3}/s},Cse^{-cr_{4}s^{-1/2}}<Ce^{-c\alpha}
), we conclude that
\mathbb{P}[\mathcal{E}_{4}]>1-Ce^{-c\alpha}
.
∎

7
In probability convergence of empirical environments

In this section we prove in probability convergence versions of the main results Theorem
1.1
and Theorem
1.2
.
The semi-infinite geodesic one (Theorem
7.1
below) follows quickly from the convergence of TASEP as seen from an isolated second-class particle (Proposition
2.7
or Theorem
1.7
), and ergodicity of the stationary process (Proposition
2.2
).
The finite geodesic one (Theorem
7.3
) is via geometric arguments, specifically, covering finite geodesics by semi-infinite geodesics.

7.1
Semi-infinite geodesics

We start with convergence along semi-infinite geodesics and giving a weak version of Theorem
1.2
.

Theorem 7.1
.

For any bounded continuous function
f:\mathbb{R}^{\mathbb{Z}^{2}}\times\{0,1\}^{\mathbb{Z}^{2}}\to\mathbb{R}
, we have
\mu_{\mathbf{0};r}(f)\to\nu(f)
in probability as
r\to\infty
.

We let
(\eta_{t}^{*})_{t\geq 0}
be the process of TASEP starting from i.i.d. Bernoulli
(\rho)
on
\mathbb{Z}\setminus\{0\}
, and
\eta_{0}^{*}(0)=*
.
Then recall (from Section
2.2
) that
\eta_{t}^{*}(l_{t}+\cdot)\sim\Phi_{t}
, for
l_{t}
being the location of the second-class particle at time
t
.
We also let
\boldsymbol{\zeta}^{*}=(\zeta^{*}_{t})_{t\in\mathbb{R}}
to be the stationary process of TASEP as seen from an isolated second-class, i.e. for each
t
we have
\zeta^{*}_{t}\sim\Psi
(defined in Section
2
).

For any process
P=(P_{w})_{w\in\mathbb{R}}
and
t\in\mathbb{R}
, we denote
\mathscr{T}_{t}P
as the process
(P_{t+w})_{w\in\mathbb{R}}
.
By Lemmas
3.7
and
4.1
, we can deduce Theorem
7.1
from the following result.
To make things well-defined, we let
\boldsymbol{\eta}^{*}=(\eta_{t}^{*}(l_{t}+\cdot))_{t\in\mathbb{R}}
, such that
\eta_{t}^{*}=\eta_{0}^{*}
and
l_{t}=0
for each
t<0
.
Let
\{0,1,*\}^{\mathbb{Z}\times\mathbb{R}}
be equipped with the product topology.

Proposition 7.2
.

For any bounded and continuous function
f:\{0,1,*\}^{\mathbb{Z}\times\mathbb{R}}\to\mathbb{R}
, we have

T^{-1}\int_{0}^{T}f(\mathscr{T}_{t}\boldsymbol{\eta}^{*})dt\to\mathbb{E}[f(\boldsymbol{\zeta}^{*})]

in probability as
T\to\infty
.

By Birkhoff’s Ergodic Theorem, this proposition follows from Proposition
2.7
or Theorem
1.7
, and Proposition
2.2
.

Proof of Proposition
7.2
.

Without loss of generality we assume that
0\leq f\leq 1
, and for some
s>0
it is measurable with respect to the
\sigma
-algebra generated by
A\times\{0,1\}^{\mathbb{Z}\times(-\infty,-s)\cup(s,\infty)}
for all measurable
A\subset\{0,1\}^{\mathbb{Z}\times[-s,s]}
.
Take any
\delta>0
, then by Birkhoff’s Ergodic Theorem and Proposition
2.2
, we can find
r
large enough such that

\mathbb{P}\left[\left|r^{-1}\int_{0}^{r}f(\mathscr{T}_{t}\boldsymbol{\zeta}^{*})dt-\mathbb{E}[f(\boldsymbol{\zeta}^{*})]\right|>\delta\right]<\delta
.

For each
t\geq 0
, denote
\chi_{t}=\mathds{1}\left[\left|r^{-1}\int_{t+s}^{t+s+r}f(\mathscr{T}_{w}\boldsymbol{\eta}^{*})dw-\mathbb{E}[f(\boldsymbol{\zeta}^{*})]\right|\geq\delta\right]
.
Let
F:\eta\mapsto\mathbb{E}[\chi_{t}\mid\eta_{t}^{*}=\eta]
, then this
F
is the same for all
t\geq 0
, and is an upper semi-continuous function on the space
\{\eta:\eta(0)=*,\eta(x)\in\{0,1\},\forall x\neq 0\}\subset\{0,1,*\}^{\mathbb{Z}}
since
f
is continuous.
Then by Theorem
1.7
we have

\limsup_{N\to\infty}N^{-1}\sum_{i=0}^{N-1}\mathbb{E}[\chi_{ir}]=\limsup_{N\to\infty}N^{-1}\sum_{i=0}^{N-1}\mathbb{E}[F(\eta_{ir}^{*})]\leq\mathbb{E}[F(\zeta_{0}^{*})]\\
=\mathbb{P}\left[\left|r^{-1}\int_{0}^{r}f(\mathscr{T}_{t}\boldsymbol{\zeta}^{*})dt-\mathbb{E}[f(\boldsymbol{\zeta}^{*})]\right|\geq\delta\right]<\delta.

This implies that for any
N
large enough, we have
\mathbb{P}[\sum_{i=0}^{N-1}\chi_{ir}>\sqrt{\delta}N]<\sqrt{\delta}
, thus

\mathbb{P}\left[\left|(Nr)^{-1}\int_{s}^{Nr+s}f(\mathscr{T}_{t}\boldsymbol{\eta}^{*})dt-\mathbb{E}[f(\boldsymbol{\zeta}^{*})]\right|>\sqrt{\delta}+\delta\right]<\sqrt{\delta},

which implies our conclusion since
\delta>0
is arbitrary.
∎

7.2
From semi-infinite geodesics to point-to-point geodesics

From the in probability convergence along semi-infinite geodesics (Theorem
7.1
), we deduce the following in probability convergence along finite geodesics. It can be viewed as a weak version of Theorem
1.1
.

Recall (from Section
5
) that we let
\langle a,b\rangle_{\rho}=\left(\left\lfloor\frac{2(1-\rho)^{2}a}{\rho^{2}+(1-\rho)^{2}}\right\rfloor+b,\left\lceil\frac{2\rho^{2}a}{\rho^{2}+(1-\rho)^{2}}\right\rceil-b\right)
.
Since
\rho
is fixed, for the rest of this paper we also write
\langle a,b\rangle=\langle a,b\rangle_{\rho}
.

Theorem 7.3
.

Let
\{b_{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
be a sequence of integers such that
\limsup_{n\to\infty}n^{-2/3}|b_{n}|<\infty
.
Then for any bounded continuous function
f:\mathbb{R}^{\mathbb{Z}^{2}}\times\{0,1\}^{\mathbb{Z}^{2}}\to\mathbb{R}
, we have
\mu_{\mathbf{0},\langle n,b_{n}\rangle}(f)\to\nu(f)
in probability as
n\to\infty
.

We now explain the strategy of proving this theorem.
The general idea is to cover the finite geodesic
\Gamma_{\mathbf{0},\langle n,b_{n}\rangle}
with a semi-infinite geodesic. More precisely, for any
\epsilon>0
, we construct an event that depends only on the i.i.d. random weights
\xi
on or above
\mathbb{L}_{n}
, such that (1) this event happens with positive probability (lower bounded uniformly in
n
) and (2) assuming this event, with high probability a
1-\epsilon
portion of
\Gamma_{\mathbf{0},\langle n,b_{n}\rangle}
is contained in
\Gamma_{\mathbf{0}}
.
Then by Theorem
7.1
, conditioned on this event the empirical environment
\mu_{\mathbf{0},\langle n,b_{n}\rangle}
would be ‘
\epsilon
-close’ to
\nu
, with high probability for
n
large enough.
On the other hand, since
\mu_{\mathbf{0},\langle n,b_{n}\rangle}
depends mainly on the random i.i.d. weights
\xi
below
\mathbb{L}_{n}
, it is roughly ‘independent’ of the constructed event, so it would always be close to
\nu
, with high probability for
n
large enough.

We start by describing the event. Recall
\mathbf{B}
(and also
\mathbf{G}
), the Busemann function in direction
\boldsymbol{\rho}
. The event basically says that the Busemann function
\mathbf{B}(\langle n,b_{n}+b\rangle,\langle n,b_{n}\rangle)
decays fast when
b
is slightly away from
0
. By Lemma
3.1
this can force
\Gamma_{\mathbf{0}}
to intersect
\mathbb{L}_{n}
near
\langle n,b_{n}+b\rangle
, and that
\Gamma_{\mathbf{0},\langle n,b_{n}\rangle}
overlaps with
\Gamma_{\mathbf{0}}
can be deduced using coalescence and
ordering of geodesics (Proposition
5.4
and Lemma
5.1
).

We now formally define this event and study its probability. For simplicity of notations, we shift it by
-\langle n,b_{n}\rangle
and look at the Busemann function on
\mathbb{L}_{0}
.
Let
\mathcal{E}_{h,n}
denote the following event: for any
b\in\mathbb{Z}
with
h^{-1}n^{2/3}<|b|<hn^{2/3}
, there is
\mathbf{G}(\langle 0,b\rangle)+b(\rho^{-1}-(1-\rho)^{-1})>hn^{1/3}
;
and for
b\in\mathbb{Z}
with
|b|\geq hn^{2/3}
, there is

\mathbf{G}(\langle 0,b\rangle)+b(\rho^{-1}-(1-\rho)^{-1})>-|b|n^{-1/3}
.
We show that its probability is lower bounded uniformly in
n
,

Lemma 7.4
.

For any
h>1
, there is
\delta>0
such that
\mathbb{P}[\mathcal{E}_{h,n}]>\delta
for all
n
large enough.

Proof.

Denote
F(b)=-\mathbf{G}(\langle 0,b\rangle)-b(\rho^{-1}-(1-\rho)^{-1})
, then
F
is a (two-sided) random walk, where each step is centered with exponential tail.
By independence of all the steps, we have

\begin{split}\mathbb{P}[\mathcal{E}_{h,n}]\geq&\mathbb{P}\left[\max_{h^{-1}n^{2/3}<|b|<hn^{2/3}}F(b)<-hn^{1/3}\right]\\
&\times\mathbb{P}\left[\max_{b\geq hn^{2/3}}F(b)-F(\lfloor hn^{2/3}\rfloor)-bn^{-1/3}<hn^{1/3}\right]\\
&\times\mathbb{P}\left[\max_{b\leq-hn^{2/3}}F(b)-F(-\lfloor hn^{2/3}\rfloor)+bn^{-1/3}<hn^{1/3}\right].\end{split}

As the process
F
converges to a (two-sided) Brownian motion (weakly in the uniform topology) in compact sets, the first factor in the right-hand side is lower bounded by a positive constant.
We next lower bound the factor in the second line, and the third line could be lower bounded in a similar way.
The second line is at least

\begin{split}\mathbb{P}\left[\max_{b\in\mathbb{N}}F(b)-bn^{-1/3}<hn^{1/3}\right]\geq&\mathbb{P}\left[\max_{b\in\llbracket 0,In^{2/3}\rrbracket}F(b)-bn^{-1/3}<hn^{1/3}\right]\\
&-\sum_{i=I}^{\infty}\mathbb{P}\left[\max_{b\in\llbracket in^{2/3},(i+1)n^{2/3}\rrbracket}F(b)\geq(i+h)n^{1/3}\right].\end{split}

where
I
is a large integer.
As
n\to\infty
, the first term in the right-hand side converges to the probability that a Brownian motion is bounded below a (sloped) line in
[0,I]
, and such probability is lower bounded uniformly in
I
.
For the sum in the second line, the term for each
i
is upper bounded by

\begin{split}&\mathbb{P}\left[F(\lceil in^{2/3}\rceil)\geq(i+h)n^{1/3}/2\right]+\mathbb{P}\left[\max_{b\in\llbracket 0,n^{2/3}\rrbracket}F(b)\geq(i+h)n^{1/3}/2\right]\\
\leq&\mathbb{P}\left[F(\lceil in^{2/3}\rceil)\geq(i+h)n^{1/3}/2\right]+2\mathbb{P}\left[F(\lfloor n^{2/3}\rfloor)\geq(i+h)n^{1/3}/2\right],\end{split}

where the inequality is by the reflection principle.
By a Bernstein type estimate for the sum of independent random variables with exponential tails, this could be bounded by
Ce^{-ci}
for some
c,C>0
, independent of
n
. Thus by taking
I
large enough the conclusion follows.
∎

\mathcal{E}_{h,n}^{\prime}
\mathcal{A}_{h,n},\mathcal{B}_{h,n}
\mathbb{L}_{\lfloor(1-h^{-1})n\rfloor}
\mathbb{L}_{n}
\mathbf{0}
\langle n,b_{n}\rangle
\Gamma_{\mathbf{0},\langle n,\lfloor b_{n}-h^{-1}n^{2/3}\rfloor\rangle}
\Gamma_{\mathbf{0},\langle n,\lceil b_{n}+h^{-1}n^{2/3}\rceil\rangle}
\Gamma_{\mathbf{0}}

Figure 14
:
An illustration of the proof of Theorem
7.3
. The event
\mathcal{E}_{h,n}^{\prime}
is on the spiky behaviour of the Busemann function, the event
\mathcal{A}_{h,n}
is on passage times from
\mathbf{0}
to
\mathbb{L}_{n}
, and the event
\mathcal{B}_{h,n}
is on coalescence of geodesics.
Under their intersection, most of
\Gamma_{\mathbf{0},\langle n,b_{n}\rangle}
is also in
\Gamma_{\mathbf{0}}
.
The events
\mathcal{A}_{h,n}
and
\mathcal{B}_{h,n}
happen with high probability, and
\mathcal{E}_{h,n}^{\prime}
happens with positive probability lower bounded uniformly in
n
.
The event
\mathcal{E}_{h,n}^{\prime}
depends only on
\xi
in the yellow region, while
\mathcal{A}_{h,n}
and
\mathcal{B}_{h,n}
depend only on
\xi
in the remaining region (and roughly so does
\mu_{\mathbf{0},\langle n,b_{n}\rangle}
).

Proof of Theorem
7.3
.

It suffices to show that, for any
s\in\mathbb{N}
and any continuous
f:\mathbb{R}^{\llbracket-s,s\rrbracket^{2}}\times\{0,1\}^{\llbracket-s,s\rrbracket^{2}}\to[0,1]
, regarded as a function on
\mathbb{R}^{\mathbb{Z}^{2}}\times\{0,1\}^{\mathbb{Z}^{2}}
, there is
\mu_{\mathbf{0},\langle n,b_{n}\rangle}(f)\to\nu(f)
in probability.

In this proof we use
c,C>0
to denote small and large enough constants, whose values may change from line to line.
We then have that
|b_{n}|<Cn^{2/3}
for any
n\in\mathbb{N}
.
For simplicity of notations we denote
T_{u,v}^{\bullet}=T_{u,v}-\xi(v)
for any vertices
u\leq v
.

We denote
\mathcal{E}_{h,n}^{\prime}
as
\mathcal{E}_{h,n}
translated by
\langle n,b_{n}\rangle
, i.e.
\mathcal{E}_{h,n}^{\prime}
is the event where

\begin{split}\mathbf{B}(\langle n,b\rangle,\langle n,b_{n}\rangle)<&(b-b_{n})(\rho^{-1}-(1-\rho)^{-1})-hn^{1/3},\;\text{ for any }h^{-1}n^{2/3}<|b-b_{n}|<hn^{2/3},\\
\mathbf{B}(\langle n,b\rangle,\langle n,b_{n}\rangle)<&(b-b_{n})(\rho^{-1}-(1-\rho)^{-1})+|b-b_{n}|n^{-1/3},\;\text{ for any }|b-b_{n}|\geq hn^{2/3}.\end{split}

Denote
\langle n,b_{n}^{\prime}\rangle
as the intersection of
\Gamma_{\mathbf{0}}
with
\mathbb{L}_{n}
.
Take any
\epsilon>0
.
By Theorem
7.1
, for any
n
large enough (depending on
\epsilon,f
), we have

\mathbb{P}[|\mu_{\mathbf{0},\langle n,b_{n}^{\prime}\rangle}(f)-\nu(f)|<\epsilon]>1-\epsilon.

By Lemma
7.4
, when
\epsilon
is taken small enough depending on
h
, we have

\mathbb{P}[|\mu_{\mathbf{0},\langle n,b_{n}^{\prime}\rangle}(f)-\nu(f)|<\epsilon\mid\mathcal{E}_{h,n}^{\prime}]>1-\sqrt{\epsilon}

(7.1)

for any
n
large enough (depending on
h,\epsilon,f
).

We next study the overlap between
\Gamma_{\mathbf{0}}
and
\Gamma_{\mathbf{0},\langle n,b_{n}\rangle}
, under the event
\mathcal{E}_{h,n}^{\prime}
.
We denote
\mathcal{A}_{h,n}
as the following event:
for any
b\in\mathbb{Z}
, we have

•

T_{\mathbf{0},\langle n,b\rangle}^{\bullet}+b(\rho^{-1}-(1-\rho)^{-1})>\mathbb{E}[T_{\mathbf{0},\langle n,0\rangle}]-hn^{1/3}/2
, if
|b-b_{n}|\leq h^{-1}n^{2/3}
;

•

T_{\mathbf{0},\langle n,b\rangle}^{\bullet}+b(\rho^{-1}-(1-\rho)^{-1})<\mathbb{E}[T_{\mathbf{0},\langle n,0\rangle}]+hn^{1/3}/2
, if
h^{-1}n^{2/3}<|b-b_{n}|<hn^{2/3}
;

•

T_{\mathbf{0},\langle n,b\rangle}^{\bullet}+b(\rho^{-1}-(1-\rho)^{-1})<\mathbb{E}[T_{\mathbf{0},\langle n,0\rangle}]-hn^{1/3}/2-|b-b_{n}|n^{-1/3}
, if
|b-b_{n}|\geq hn^{2/3}
.

We have
\mathbb{P}[\mathcal{A}_{h,n}]>1-e^{-ch}
for
n
and
h
large enough.
This can be deduced by applying (
5.1
) in Theorem
5.2
to
T_{\mathbf{0},\langle n,b\rangle}
for each
b\in\llbracket-n,n\rrbracket
with
|b|>(\rho^{2}\wedge(1-\rho)^{2})n
, and splitting
\{\langle n,b\rangle:b\in\llbracket-(\rho^{2}\wedge(1-\rho)^{2})n,(\rho^{2}\wedge(1-\rho)^{2})n\rrbracket\}
into segments of length
n^{2/3}
and using Proposition
5.3
with each one of them.

We also denote
\mathcal{B}_{h,n}
as the following event:

\Gamma_{\mathbf{0},\langle n,\lfloor b_{n}-h^{-1}n^{2/3}\rfloor\rangle}\cap\mathbb{L}_{\lfloor(1-h^{-1})n\rfloor}=\Gamma_{\mathbf{0},\langle n,\lceil b_{n}+h^{-1}n^{2/3}\rceil\rangle}\cap\mathbb{L}_{\lfloor(1-h^{-1})n\rfloor}.

By Proposition
5.4
, we have
\mathbb{P}[\mathcal{B}_{h,n}]>1-Ch^{-1/3}
, for
h<cn^{2/3}
and
h
large enough.

Note that
\mathcal{A}_{h,n}
and
\mathcal{B}_{h,n}
only depend on the i.i.d. random weights
\xi
below
\mathbb{L}_{n}
, and
\mathcal{E}_{h,n}^{\prime}
only depends on
\xi
on or above
\mathbb{L}_{n}
, so the events
\mathcal{A}_{h,n},\mathcal{B}_{h,n}
are independent of
\mathcal{E}_{h,n}^{\prime}
(see Figure
14
).
Using that
\mathbb{P}[\mathcal{A}_{h,n}]>1-e^{-ch}
,
\mathbb{P}[\mathcal{B}_{h,n}]>1-Ch^{-1/3}
, and (
7.1
), for
n
large enough (depending on
h,\epsilon,f
) we have

\mathbb{P}[\mathcal{A}_{h,n},\mathcal{B}_{h,n},|\mu_{\mathbf{0},\langle n,b_{n}^{\prime}\rangle}(f)-\nu(f)|<\epsilon\mid\mathcal{E}_{h,n}^{\prime}]>1-\sqrt{\epsilon}-e^{-ch}-Ch^{-1/3}.

Under
\mathcal{A}_{h,n}\cap\mathcal{E}_{h,n}^{\prime}
,
we have

T_{\mathbf{0},\langle n,b\rangle}^{\bullet}+\mathbf{B}(\langle n,b\rangle,\langle n,b_{n}\rangle)<T_{\mathbf{0},\langle n,b_{n}\rangle}^{\bullet},

for any
b\in\mathbb{Z}
,
|b-b_{n}|>h^{-1}n^{2/3}
.
Thus there must be
|b_{n}-b_{n}^{\prime}|\leq h^{-1}n^{2/3}
by Lemma
3.1
.
Then under
\mathcal{A}_{h,n}\cap\mathcal{B}_{h,n}\cap\mathcal{E}_{h,n}^{\prime}
, we must have
\Gamma_{\mathbf{0},\langle n,b_{n}\rangle}\cap\mathbb{L}_{\lfloor(1-h^{-1})n\rfloor}=\Gamma_{\mathbf{0},\langle n,b_{n}^{\prime}\rangle}\cap\mathbb{L}_{\lfloor(1-h^{-1})n\rfloor}
by ordering of geodesics (Lemma
5.1
), and
|\mu_{\mathbf{0},\langle n,b_{n}^{\prime}\rangle}(f)-\nu(f)|<\epsilon
implies that

|\mu_{\mathbf{0},\langle n,b_{n}\rangle}(f)-\nu(f)|<\epsilon+h^{-1}.

So we have

\mathbb{P}[|\mu_{\mathbf{0},\langle n,b_{n}\rangle}(f)-\nu(f)|<\epsilon+h^{-1}\mid\mathcal{E}_{h,n}^{\prime}]>1-\sqrt{\epsilon}-e^{-ch}-Ch^{-1/3}.

Note that
\Gamma_{\mathbf{0},\langle n,b_{n}\rangle}
is determined by the weights
\xi
below
\mathbb{L}_{n}
, so it is independent of
\mathcal{E}_{h,n}^{\prime}
. For each
v\in\Gamma_{\mathbf{0},\langle n,b_{n}\rangle}
with
d(v)<2n-2s
,
f(v)
is determined by the weights
\xi
in
v+\llbracket-s,s\rrbracket^{2}
, so it is also independent of
\mathcal{E}_{h,n}^{\prime}
.
Thus we conclude that

\mathbb{P}[|\mu_{\mathbf{0},\langle n,b_{n}\rangle}(f)-\nu(f)|<\epsilon+h^{-1}+s/n]>1-\sqrt{\epsilon}-e^{-ch}-Ch^{-1/3}

for any
n
large enough (depending on
h,\epsilon,f
). Since
h
can be taken arbitrarily large and
\epsilon
is any number small enough depending on
h
,
we conclude that
\mu_{\mathbf{0},\langle n,b_{n}\rangle}(f)\to\nu(f)
in probability.
∎

8
Parallelogram uniform covering

The goal of this section is to prove the following upgraded version of Theorem
7.3
. It will be the key input for the next two sections.

Proposition 8.1
.

For any
h>0
,
s\in\mathbb{N}
, and any bounded continuous continuous
f:\mathbb{R}^{\llbracket-s,s\rrbracket^{2}}\times\{0,1\}^{\llbracket-s,s\rrbracket^{2}}\to\mathbb{R}
, regarded as a function on
\mathbb{R}^{\mathbb{Z}^{2}}\times\{0,1\}^{\mathbb{Z}^{2}}
, we have

\max_{a,b\in\mathbb{Z},|a|,|b|<hn^{2/3}}\mu_{\langle 0,a\rangle,\langle n,b\rangle}(f),\min_{a,b\in\mathbb{Z},|a|,|b|<hn^{2/3}}\mu_{\langle 0,a\rangle,\langle n,b\rangle}(f)\to\nu(f),

in probability.

For simplicity of notations, below we write the proof for
\rho=1/2
, while the general
\rho
case follows essentially verbatim.

We now explain our strategy.
We will take two families of vertices,
\mathfrak{P}_{1}
and
\mathfrak{P}_{2}
, around the segment connecting
\langle 0,-hn^{2/3}\rangle
and
\langle 0,hn^{2/3}\rangle
and the segment connecting
\langle n,-hn^{2/3}\rangle
and
\langle n,hn^{2/3}\rangle
, respectively.
Both
\mathfrak{P}_{1}
and
\mathfrak{P}_{2}
are finite, in the sense that their sizes do not increase as
n\to\infty
.
Then by Theorem
7.3
, when
n
is large enough, with high probability, for any
u\in\mathfrak{P}_{1}
and
v\in\mathfrak{P}_{2}
,
\mu_{u,v}(f)
is close to
\nu(f)
.
We will show that with high probability, for any
|a|,|b|<hn^{2/3}
, the geodesic
\Gamma_{\langle 0,a\rangle,\langle n,b\rangle}
is mostly covered by some
\Gamma_{u,v}
with
u\in\mathfrak{P}_{1}
and
v\in\mathfrak{P}_{2}
, thus
\mu_{\langle 0,a\rangle,\langle n,b\rangle}(f)
is also close to
\nu(f)
.

The main task to do is to establish the covering statement.
To motivate our arguments, we start with the following attempt.
For
a^{-}<a^{+}
and
b^{-}<b^{+}
, if the geodesics
\Gamma_{\langle 0,a^{-}\rangle,\langle n,b^{-}\rangle}
and
\Gamma_{\langle 0,a^{+}\rangle,\langle n,b^{+}\rangle}
coalescence near both ends, then they must mostly stay together; and by ordering of geodesics (Lemma
5.1
), for any
a^{-}<a<a^{+}
and
b^{-}<b<b^{+}
, the geodesic
\Gamma_{\langle 0,a\rangle,\langle n,b\rangle}
must be covered by
\Gamma_{\langle 0,a^{-}\rangle,\langle n,b^{-}\rangle}
, except for a small portion.
By estimates on coalescence of geodesics (e.g. Proposition
5.4
), if we let
b^{+}-b^{-}=a^{+}-a^{-}
be in the order of
\delta_{0}n^{2/3}
(for some small
\delta_{0}>0
), the probability for
\Gamma_{\langle 0,a^{-}\rangle,\langle n,b^{-}\rangle}
and
\Gamma_{\langle 0,a^{+}\rangle,\langle n,b^{+}\rangle}
to stay disjoint within order
n
distance from their endpoints is in the order of
\delta_{0}
.
Now we take
\mathfrak{P}_{1}
and
\mathfrak{P}_{2}
to be contained in the segment connecting
\langle 0,-hn^{2/3}\rangle
and
\langle 0,hn^{2/3}\rangle
and the segment connecting
\langle n,-hn^{2/3}\rangle
and
\langle n,hn^{2/3}\rangle
, respectively.
Let these vertices split these two segments into
h\delta_{0}^{-1}
many small segments, each of length
\delta_{0}n^{-2/3}
.
By taking a union bound over all pairs of such small segments, we conclude that the probability of existing some
\Gamma_{\langle 0,a\rangle,\langle n,b\rangle}
not being mostly covered (by one geodesic with two endpoints in
\mathfrak{P}_{1}
and
\mathfrak{P}_{2}
) is upper bounded by
(\delta_{0}^{-1})^{2}\delta_{0}
, which is too large.

To resolve this issue, we need to get a better bound on the probability of the following event:
there exist some
a^{-}<a<a^{+}
and
b^{-}<b<b^{+}
, such that the geodesic
\Gamma_{\langle 0,a\rangle,\langle n,b\rangle}
is not mostly covered by any geodesic with endpoints in
\mathfrak{P}_{1}
and
\mathfrak{P}_{2}
.
If this probability could be upper bounded by
\delta_{0}^{2+\epsilon}
for some
\epsilon>0
(rather than
\delta_{0}
), then by a union bound and sending
\delta_{0}\to 0
, the conclusion follows.
Towards this, we need to take
\mathfrak{P}_{1}
and
\mathfrak{P}_{2}
larger (but still finite).
Instead of having them contained in
\mathbb{L}_{0}
and
\mathbb{L}_{n}
, we let
\mathfrak{P}_{1}
and
\mathfrak{P}_{2}
have
h\delta_{0}^{-1}\times\delta_{0}^{-1}
vertices in the rectangles
\{u:0\leq d(u)\leq 2n/3,-2hn^{2/3}\leq ad(u)\leq 2hn^{2/3}\}
and
\{u:4n/3\leq d(u)\leq 2n,-2hn^{2/3}\leq ad(u)\leq 2hn^{2/3}\}
, respectively.
Fix some small
\kappa>0
.
Using ordering of geodesics (Lemma
5.1
), and a union bound, the above task can roughly be reduced to proving the following statement.
For given
a^{-},a^{+}
and
b^{-},b_{+}
that are contained in
[-hn^{2/3},hn^{2/3}]
with
b^{+}-b^{-}=a^{+}-a^{-}
in the order of
\delta_{0}n^{2/3}
,
the following event happens with probability in the order of at most
\delta_{0}^{2+\epsilon}
for some
\epsilon>0
:
there exist
a^{-}<a<a^{+}
and
b^{-}<b<b^{+}
, such that for any
u\in\mathfrak{P}_{1}
and
v\in\mathfrak{P}_{2}
in the same side of
\Gamma_{\langle 0,a\rangle,\langle n,b\rangle}
,
\Gamma_{\langle 0,a\rangle,\langle n,b\rangle}\cap\Gamma_{u,v}
contains no vertex below
\mathbb{L}_{2\kappa n}
.

Now let’s consider the scenario where the above event happens.
Take any
v\in\mathfrak{P}_{2}
that is within distance
\delta_{0}n^{2/3}
to
\Gamma_{\langle 0,a\rangle,\langle n,b\rangle}
.
We find vertices
u_{1},u_{2},u_{3},u_{4},u_{5}
in
\mathfrak{P}_{1}
, such that (1) they are between
\mathbb{L}_{\kappa n}
and
\mathbb{L}_{2\kappa n}
; (2) these vertices are in the same side of
\Gamma_{\langle 0,a\rangle,\langle n,b\rangle}
as
v
; (3) each is within distance
\delta_{0}n^{2/3}
to
\Gamma_{\langle 0,a\rangle,\langle n,b\rangle}
.
Consider the geodesics from each of these vertices to
v
: these geodesics are disjoint from
\Gamma_{\langle 0,a\rangle,\langle n,b\rangle}
below
\mathbb{L}_{2\kappa n}
, by the above event. We can show that (with high probability), any two geodesics cannot stay close to each other while being disjoint for a long distance.
By choosing the vertices
u_{5},u_{4},u_{3},u_{2},u_{1}
sequentially and in a multi-scale way (see Figure
17
below for an illustration), we can actually find
\alpha_{0}
with
\kappa<\alpha_{0}\in 2\kappa
, such that for
\Gamma_{u_{i},v}
with
i=1,2,3,4,5
and
\Gamma_{\langle 0,a\rangle,\langle n,b\rangle}
, their intersections with
\mathbb{L}_{\alpha_{0}n}
are far from each other (with distances in the order of at least
\delta_{0}^{1/150}n^{2/3}
).

However, using
\Gamma_{\langle 0,a\rangle,\langle n,b\rangle}
and each
\Gamma_{u_{i},v}
, (with high probability) one can construct a path from
\langle 0,a^{-}\rangle
to
\langle n,b^{-}\rangle
, and the difference between its passage time and
T_{\langle 0,a^{-}\rangle,\langle n,b^{-}\rangle}
is at most in the order of
\delta_{0}^{1/2}n^{1/3}
.
Indeed, one can just mainly use the path of
\Gamma_{u_{i},v}
, and switch to
\Gamma_{\langle 0,a\rangle,\langle n,b\rangle}
only near
u_{i}
and
v
, and switch to
\langle 0,a^{-}\rangle
and
\langle n,b^{-}\rangle
near the ends.
One can also just mainly use the path
\Gamma_{\langle 0,a\rangle,\langle n,b\rangle}
and switch to
\langle 0,a^{-}\rangle
and
\langle n,b^{-}\rangle
near the ends.
This way we get in total
6
paths from
\langle 0,a^{-}\rangle
to
\langle n,b^{-}\rangle
, each with total passage time at least
T_{\langle 0,a^{-}\rangle,\langle n,b^{-}\rangle}-\delta_{0}^{1/2}n^{1/3}
; and they intersect
\mathbb{L}_{\alpha_{0}n}
at vertices far away from each other.
Now consider the optimal passage time from
\langle 0,a^{-}\rangle
to
\langle n,b^{-}\rangle
passing through
\langle\alpha_{0}n,b^{\prime}\rangle
, as a function of
b^{\prime}
. This is roughly the sum of two independent point-to-line last-passage profiles (see Section
8.1
below).
Its scaling limit is known to behave like a Brownian motion, and the event that there are
6
paths with near optimal passage times is reduced to that, for a Brownian motion in a compact interval one can find
6
points such that their distances are at least in the order of
\delta_{0}^{1/150}
, and the Brownian motion values at these points are at least the maximum (of the Brownian motion) minus
\delta_{0}^{1/2}
.
This event has probability in the order of at most
(\delta_{0}^{1/2-1/300})^{5}=\delta_{0}^{5/2-1/60}
, which is smaller
\delta_{0}^{2+\epsilon}
as needed (and this is also why we need to find
5
alternative paths).

We now explain the organization of the remaining of this section.
We will first list some useful ingredients that will be useful in carrying out the above plan. The proofs of some of these ingredients are delayed to Section
8.1
and Section
8.2
.
Then we will define several events, each with a small probability. The main arguments are contained in the proof of Lemma
8.6
below, where we show that under the intersection of the complements of these events, every
\Gamma_{\langle 0,a\rangle,\langle n,b\rangle}
is mostly covered by one geodesic in a finite family.
Finally we deduce Proposition
8.1
using Lemma
8.6
.

Ingredients:

The first one concerns continuity of the function
(a,b)\mapsto T_{\langle 0,a\rangle,\langle n,b\rangle}
.

Lemma 8.2
.

There exist constants
c,C>0
such that the following is true.
For
h>0
,
0<\theta<1
, and
t>1
, we have

\mathbb{P}\left[\max_{\begin{subarray}{c}|a|,|a^{\prime}|,|b|,|b^{\prime}|<hn^{2/3}\\
|a-a^{\prime}|,|b-b^{\prime}|<\theta n^{2/3}\end{subarray}}|T_{\langle 0,a\rangle,\langle n,b\rangle}-T_{\langle 0,a^{\prime}\rangle,\langle n,b^{\prime}\rangle}|>t\theta^{1/2-0.01}n^{1/3}+Ch\theta n^{1/3}\right]<Che^{-ct}

when
n
is large enough (depending on
h,\theta,t
).

The proof of this lemma will be given in Section
8.1
.

We next state a bound on transversal fluctuations of geodesics.
It actually immediately follows from the results in Section
5
, and we state it here mainly for the convenience of the proof of Proposition
8.1
.
For vertices
u\leq v
, and
0\leq l\leq d(v)-d(u)
,
t>1
, let
\mathcal{T}^{u,v}_{l,t}
be the event where
\Gamma_{u,v}
below
u+\mathbb{L}_{l}
is not contained in a rectangle of width
2tl^{2/3}
, or
\Gamma_{u,v}
above
v-\mathbb{L}_{l}
is not contained in a rectangle of width
2tl^{2/3}
(see Figure
15
).
Formally, we let
\Gamma_{u,v}
be the event where there exists
w\in\Gamma_{u,v}
with
d(u)\leq d(w)\leq d(u)+2l
and
|ad(w)-ad(u)|\geq 2tl^{2/3}
,
or with
d(v)-2l\leq d(w)\leq d(v)
and
|ad(w)-ad(v)|\geq 2tl^{2/3}
.

\mathbb{L}_{l}
\mathbb{L}_{n-l}
\mathbf{0}
\langle n,b\rangle

Figure 15
:
The complement of the event
\mathcal{T}_{l,t}^{\mathbf{0},\langle n,b\rangle}
: the geodesic
\Gamma_{\mathbf{0},\langle n,b\rangle}
is restricted within the green boxes with width
tl^{2/3}
, below
\mathbb{L}_{l}
or above
\mathbb{L}_{n-l}
.

Lemma 8.3
.

For
h>0
, there exist constants
c,C>0
such that the following is true.
For any
0\leq l\leq n
large enough, and
|b|<hn^{2/3}
,
t>1
, we have
\mathbb{P}[\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{0},\langle n,b\rangle}_{l,t}]<Ce^{-ct^{3}}
.

This lemma can be obtained by applying Corollary
5.9
twice, and we omit its proof.

Our next lemma establishes that, for a geodesic and a path with a ‘near-optimal’ passage time, it is unlikely for them to stay together for a while but remain disjoint.

For any vertices
u\leq v
, and
M,l\in\mathbb{N},m\in\mathbb{Z}
with
d(u)\leq 2m<2m+2Ml\leq d(v)
, and a small enough parameter
c_{0}>0
, we denote
\mathcal{D}_{M,l,m}^{u,v}
as the following event (see Figure
16
): there exists an up-right path
\gamma
from
\mathbb{L}_{m}
to
\mathbb{L}_{m+Ml}
,
such that

•

\gamma
is disjoint from
\Gamma_{u,v}
.

•

The passage time of
\gamma
(i.e.
T(\gamma)
) is at least
4Ml-c_{0}Ml^{1/3}
.

•

For each
i=0,1,\ldots,M
,
|ad(\Gamma_{u,v}\cap\mathbb{L}_{m+il})-ad(\gamma\cap\mathbb{L}_{m+il})|<2c_{0}l^{2/3}
.

Lemma 8.4
.

There exist universal constants
c,C>0
such that the following is true.
For any
M,l,n\in\mathbb{N}
and
m,b\in\mathbb{Z}
with
l>C
,
c_{0}<c
,
|b|\leq n
, and
0\leq m<m+Ml\leq n
, we have
\mathbb{P}[\mathcal{D}_{M,l,m}^{\mathbf{0},\langle n,b\rangle}]<Ce^{-cM}
.

\mathbb{L}_{m}
\mathbb{L}_{m+l}
\cdots
\mathbb{L}_{m+Ml}
u
v
u^{\prime}
v^{\prime}
\gamma
\Gamma_{u,v}

Figure 16
:
The event
\mathcal{D}_{M,l,m}^{u,v}
: each green segment has length
<c_{0}l^{2/3}
, and
T(\gamma)\geq 4Ml-c_{0}Ml^{1/3}
.

The last ingredient we need is to bound the probability of multiple peaks in the sum of two independent point-to-line profiles.

As done in previous sections, we denote
T_{u,v}^{\bullet}=T_{u,v}-\xi(v)
for any vertices
u\leq v
(i.e. remove the weight of the last vertex).
For any vertices
u\leq v
, and
m\in\mathbb{Z}
with
d(u)\leq 2m\leq d(v)
, and
\lambda,t>0
